Nevertheless, if one would use classical splines (instead of neural network), then it would take a finite number of steps to have an error of
0
at the points
k\in\{1,...,N\}
. Therefore, one could hope that it would take as most
O(\log(n))
operation for a tanh (or ReQU) neural network to obtain an error
O(n^{-2})
.

Step 2.
Computing the operator
V
only requires a finite number of products and sums. Therefore this step takes
O(\log(n))
operations.

Step 3.
This step requires the computations of the
\hat{\psi}^{per}_{jlz}
, but for
u\in[0,1]^{d}
, and fixed
j
, there are only a finite number of
\hat{\psi}^{per}_{jlz}(u)
that are not 0. The computation of each
\hat{\psi}^{per}_{jlz}
using the neural network
\hat{\phi}=\hat{f}_{\log(n)}
from step 2, is done in a finite time. Therefore this step takes
O(\log(n))
operations.

Note that once the neural network class
\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\beta+1,n^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+d}}}_{per}
has been implemented, it is then very easy to optimize it in the GAN loss (
1.3
) as the neural network depends linearly on the weights
\hat{\alpha}_{f}(j,l,z)_{i}
.

Our choice of neural network architecture is motivated by the need of having generators with a small
\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}
norm. Indeed, it is stated in Proposition
E.3
that the neural network within
\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\beta+1,n^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+d}}}_{per}
have their
\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}
norm bounded by
O(\log(n)^{2})
. This is a key property allowing to use the interpolation inequality (Theorem
5.1
) without getting multiplicative constants larger than
O(\log(n)^{C})
. If one were to use a more classical architecture (with prescribed width and depth), they would not have a guarantee that the generator selected by the GAN will have a
\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}
norm bounded by
O(\log(n)^{C})
. Indeed, although a tanh or ReQu neural network is smooth, the norm of its derivatives can be proportional to the size of its layers. Therefore, one would have to constraint the norm of the derivatives by adding to the GAN loss (
1.3
) a cost depending on the value of the derivatives which would make the estimator not tractable.

B
Proofs of the technical tools of Section
3

In this section, we gather the proofs of Section
3
. We start by stating some preliminaries results of interpolation theory that will be used in the proofs. Then, we give the proofs of each result in the order in which they are stated in the paper.

B.1
Some useful interpolation inequalities

Let us state a general interpolation inequality that shows that we can trade of the regularity of the distributions in the
L^{2}
scalar product.

Proposition B.1
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be either
\mathbb{R}^{\eta}
or
[0,1]^{\eta}/\mathbb{Z}^{\eta}
,
h_{1}\in\mathcal{B}^{s_{1},b_{1}}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{R}^{p},1)
and
h_{2}\in\mathcal{B}^{s_{2},b_{2}}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{R}^{p},1)
for some
s_{1},s_{2},b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
. Then for
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
,
t,r\in[0,1]
,
q>1
and
1/q+1/q^{\star}=1
we have

\Big\langle h_{1},h_{2}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{R}^{p})}\leq\Big\langle\tilde{\Gamma}^{t\tau}(h_{1}),\Gamma^{(1-t)\tau}(h_{2})\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{R}^{p})}^{\frac{1}{q}}\Big\langle\tilde{\Gamma}^{-r\frac{q^{\star}}{q}\tau}(h_{1}),\Gamma^{-(1-r)\frac{q^{\star}}{q}\tau}(h_{2})\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{R}^{p})}^{\frac{1}{q^{\star}}}

where
\tilde{\Gamma}^{t\tau}(h_{1})\in\mathcal{B}^{s_{1}+t\tau,b_{1}}_{\infty,\infty}
and
\tilde{\Gamma}^{-r\frac{q^{\star}}{q}\tau}(h_{1})\in\mathcal{B}^{s_{1}-r\frac{q^{\star}}{q},b_{1}}_{\infty,\infty}
are defined by

\langle\tilde{\Gamma}^{t\tau}(h_{1})_{i},\psi_{jlz}\rangle=S(j,l,w)_{i}\langle\Gamma^{t\tau}(h_{1})_{i},\psi_{jlz}\rangle

and

\langle\tilde{\Gamma}^{-t\frac{q^{\star}}{q}\tau}(h_{1})_{i},\psi_{jlz}\rangle=S(j,l,w)_{i}\langle\Gamma^{-r\frac{q^{\star}}{q}\tau}(h_{1})_{i},\psi_{jlz}\rangle

with
S(j,l,w)_{i}\in\{-1,1\}
the sign of
\langle h_{1_{i}},\psi_{jlz}\rangle\langle h_{2_{i}},\psi_{jlz}\rangle
,
i=1,...,p
.

The proof of this proposition is a simple application of Hölder’s inequality on the wavelets coefficients of
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
. For
i\in\{1,...,p\}
and
k\in\{1,2\}
, writing
\alpha_{h_{k}}(j,l,w)_{i}=\langle h_{k_{i}},\psi_{j,l,w}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{R}^{p})}
we have

\displaystyle\Big\langle h_{1}

\displaystyle,h_{2}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{R}^{p})}=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{\eta}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{\eta}}\alpha_{h_{1}}(j,l,w)_{i}\alpha_{h_{2}}(j,l,w)_{i}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{\eta}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{\eta}}2^{j\frac{\tau}{q}}|\alpha_{h_{1}}(j,l,w)_{i}\alpha_{h_{2}}(j,l,w)_{i}|^{1/q}2^{-j\frac{\tau}{q}}|\alpha_{h_{1}}(j,l,w)_{i}\alpha_{h_{2}}(j,l,w)_{i}|^{1/q^{\star}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{\eta}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{\eta}}2^{j\tau}|\alpha_{h_{1}}(j,l,w)_{i}\alpha_{h_{2}}(j,l,w)_{i}|\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{\eta}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{\eta}}2^{-\tau\frac{q^{\star}}{q}}|\alpha_{h_{1}}(j,l,w)_{i}\alpha_{h_{2}}(j,l,w)_{i}|\right)^{\frac{1}{q^{\star}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\langle\tilde{\Gamma}^{t\tau}(h_{1}),\Gamma^{(1-t)\tau}(h_{2})\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{R}^{p})}^{\frac{1}{q}}\Big\langle\tilde{\Gamma}^{-r\frac{q^{\star}}{q}\tau}(h_{1}),\Gamma^{-(1-r)\frac{q^{\star}}{q}\tau}(h_{2})\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{R}^{p})}^{\frac{1}{q^{\star}}}.

∎

This proposition is a key result that we will use numerous times all along the paper. An useful corollary is the following.

Corollary B.2
.

Let
\mu,\nu
be two probability measures with compact supports in
\mathbb{R}^{p}
. Then for all
\theta,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}>0
with
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}
non integers such that
\theta_{1}<\theta<\theta_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{D\in\mathcal{H}^{\theta}_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X\sim\mu\\
Y\sim\nu\end{subarray}}[D(X)-D(Y)]\leq

\displaystyle C\sup\limits_{D\in\mathcal{H}^{\theta_{1}}_{1}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X\sim\mu\\
Y\sim\nu\end{subarray}}[D(X)-D(Y)]\right)^{\frac{\theta_{2}-\theta}{\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}}}

\displaystyle\times\sup\limits_{D\in\mathcal{H}^{\theta_{2}}_{1}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X\sim\mu\\
Y\sim\nu\end{subarray}}[D(X)-D(Y)]\right)^{\frac{\theta-\theta_{1}}{\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}}}.

We will use this result in the proof of minimax optimality of the GAN estimator of Section
4
. Before giving the proof of this result, let us first define the notion of envelope of a measure.

Definition B.3
.

Let
\mu
be a probability measure compactly supported on
\mathbb{R}^{p}
. For
\epsilon\in(0,1]
, we call the
\epsilon
-envelope of
\mu
, the probability measure
\mu_{\epsilon}
defined for any Borel set
A
by

\mu_{\epsilon}(A)=\frac{1}{\lambda^{p}(B^{p}(0,\epsilon))}\int_{\mathds{R}^{p}}\lambda^{p}(A\cap B(x,\epsilon)){\rm d}\mu(x).

(B.1)

Then for
\gamma>0
and
D\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}D(x)(d\mu-d\mu_{\epsilon})(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\left(D(x)-\frac{1}{\lambda^{p}(B^{p}(0,\epsilon))}\int_{B^{p}(x,\epsilon)}D(y)d\lambda^{p}(y)\right)d\mu(x)\leq\epsilon^{1\wedge\gamma}.

Furthermore,
\mu_{\epsilon}
is absolutely continuous with respect to
\lambda^{p}
and its density
f_{\mu}^{\epsilon}
equals to

\displaystyle f_{\mu}^{\epsilon}(y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda^{p}(B^{p}(0,\epsilon))}\int_{\mathds{R}^{p}}\mathds{1}_{\{y\in B^{p}(x,\epsilon)\}}{\rm d}\mu(x)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{p}(B^{p}(0,\epsilon))^{-1},

so
f_{\mu}^{\epsilon}
is bounded and therefore belongs to
L^{2}
. Let us now give the proof of Corollary
B.2
.

For
\epsilon\in(0,1]
, let
\mu_{\epsilon},\nu_{\epsilon}
be the
\epsilon
-envelope of
\mu,\nu
respectively and
f^{\epsilon}_{\mu},f_{\nu}^{\epsilon}
the densities of
\mu_{\epsilon}
and
\nu_{\epsilon}
. For all
D\in\mathcal{H}^{\theta}_{1}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mu}[D(X)]-\mathbb{E}_{Y\sim\nu}[D(Y)]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{X_{\epsilon}\sim\mu_{\epsilon}}[D(X_{\epsilon})]-\mathbb{E}_{Y_{\epsilon}\sim\nu_{\epsilon}}[D(Y_{\epsilon})]+2\epsilon^{1\wedge\theta}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}D(x)(f_{\mu}^{\epsilon}(x)-f_{\nu}^{\epsilon}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)+2\epsilon^{1\wedge\theta}.

Using Proposition
B.1
for
h_{1}=D
,
h_{2}=f_{\mu}^{\epsilon}-f_{\nu}^{\epsilon}
,
s_{1}=\theta
,
\tau=\theta-\theta_{1}
,
s_{2}=b_{1}=b_{2}=0
,
t=r=1

and
q=\frac{\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}}{\theta_{2}-\theta}
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}D(x)(f_{\mu}^{\epsilon}(x)-f_{\nu}^{\epsilon}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\theta-\theta_{1}}(D)(x)(f_{\mu}^{\epsilon}(x)-f_{\nu}^{\epsilon}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)\right)^{\frac{\theta_{2}-\theta}{\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}}}

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\theta-\theta_{2}}(D)(x)(f_{\mu}^{\epsilon}(x)-f_{\nu}^{\epsilon}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)\right)^{\frac{\theta-\theta_{1}}{\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\sup\limits_{D\in\mathcal{B}^{\theta_{1}}_{\infty,\infty}(1)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}D(x)(f_{\mu}^{\epsilon}(x)-f_{\nu}^{\epsilon}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)\right)^{\frac{\theta_{2}-\theta}{\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}}}

\displaystyle\left(\sup\limits_{D\in\mathcal{B}^{\theta_{2}}_{\infty,\infty}(1)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}D(x)(f_{\mu}^{\epsilon}(x)-f_{\nu}^{\epsilon}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)\right)^{\frac{\theta-\theta_{1}}{\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}}}.

From Lemma
3.3
we have that
\mathcal{B}^{\theta_{1}}_{\infty,\infty}(1)\subset\mathcal{H}^{\theta_{1}}_{C}
and for any
D\in\mathcal{H}^{\theta_{1}}_{C}
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}D(x)(f_{\mu}^{\epsilon}(x)-f_{\nu}^{\epsilon}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)

\displaystyle\leq 2\epsilon^{1\wedge\theta_{1}}+\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mu}[D(X)]-\mathbb{E}_{Y\sim\nu}[D(Y)].

Doing the same thing for
D\in\mathcal{H}^{\theta_{2}}_{C}
and letting
\epsilon
go to
0
, we get the result.
∎

Let us now show that we can gain some weak Besov regularity by paying a logarithmic term.

Proposition B.4
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be either
\mathbb{R}^{p}
or
\mathbb{T}^{d}
,
f\in L^{1}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{R},1)\cap L^{2}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{R})
and
g\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{1}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{R})
with
\gamma>0
. Then for all
\tau>0
,
\epsilon\in(0,1)
we have

\int_{\mathcal{X}}f(x)g(x)d\lambda_{\mathcal{X}}(x)\leq C\log(\epsilon^{-1})^{\tau}\int_{\mathcal{X}}f(x)\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-\tau}_{\epsilon}(g)(x)d\lambda_{\mathcal{X}}(x)+C_{\gamma}\epsilon

for
C_{\gamma}
such that
\mathcal{B}^{\gamma/2}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{R},1)\subset\mathcal{H}^{0}_{C_{\gamma}}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{R})
and

\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-\tau}_{\epsilon}(g)(x)=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\log(\epsilon^{-2/\gamma})}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{\text{dim}(\mathcal{X})}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{\text{dim}(\mathcal{X})}}(1+j)^{-\tau}\alpha_{g}(j,l,w)\frac{\alpha_{g}(j,l,w)\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)}{|\alpha_{g}(j,l,w)\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)|}\psi_{j,l,w}(x).

Let us write
M
the dimension of the space
\mathcal{X}
. For
k\geq 1
we have

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=k}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{M}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{M}}\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)\alpha_{g}(j,l,w)

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{j=k}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{M}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{M}}C2^{-j(\gamma+M/2)}|\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)|

\displaystyle\leq C2^{-k\gamma/2}\sum\limits_{j=k}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{M}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{M}}2^{-j(\gamma+M)/2}|\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)|

\displaystyle=C2^{-k\gamma/2}\int\kappa(x)f(x)d\lambda^{p}(x)

for

\kappa(x)=\sum\limits_{j=k}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{M}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{M}}2^{-j(\gamma+M)/2}S(j,l,w)\psi_{j,l,w}(x)

with
S(j,l,w)=\frac{\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)}{|\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)|}
.
As
\kappa\in\mathcal{B}^{\gamma/2}_{\infty,\infty}(1)\subset\mathcal{H}^{0}_{C_{\gamma}}
, we have

\int\kappa(x)f(x)d\lambda^{p}(x)\leq\|\kappa\|_{\infty}\|f\|_{L_{1}}\leq C_{\gamma}.

Let us write
\gamma_{\epsilon}=\lfloor\log_{2}(\epsilon^{-2/\gamma})\rfloor
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}f(x)g(x)d\lambda_{\mathcal{X}}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{M}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{M}}\alpha_{g}(j,l,w)\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\gamma_{\epsilon}}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{M}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{M}}\alpha_{g}(j,l,w)\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)+C_{\gamma}\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq(\gamma_{\epsilon}+1)^{\tau}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\gamma_{\epsilon}}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{M}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{M}}(1+j)^{-\tau}|\alpha_{g}(j,l,w)\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)|+C_{\gamma}\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq C\log(\epsilon^{-1})^{\tau}\int_{\mathcal{X}}f(x)\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-\tau}_{\epsilon}(g)(x)d\lambda_{\mathcal{X}}(x)+C_{\gamma}\epsilon

∎

B.2
Proof of the bias-variance trade-off for GANs (Section
3.1
)

Let us define some useful notations for the GAN estimator.

Definition B.5
.

For
D\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{1}
and
g\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{K}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})
define:

•

the discrimination score under
D
of the push-forward measure
g_{\#U}
and
g^{\star}_{\#U}
as

L(g,D):=\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}[D(g(U))-D(g^{\star}(U))],

•

the discretized version of the discrimination score

L_{n}(g,D):=\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(X_{i})-D(g(U_{i})),

with
U_{i}\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})
and
X_{i}\sim g^{\star}_{\#U}
i.i.d. random variables,
i=1,...,n
,

•

the optimal discriminator in
\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{1}
between
g_{\#U}

D^{\star}_{g}\in\argmax\limits_{D\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{1}}L(g,D),

•

the optimal discriminator in
\mathcal{D}

of the empirical approximation of the expectation

\hat{D}_{g}\in\argmax\limits_{D\in\mathcal{D}}L_{n}(g,D).

When the context is clear we will write
\mathbb{E}[D(g(U))]
instead of
\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}[D(g(U))]
.

B.2.1
Proof of Theorem
3.1

Let us state a first proposition that gives a bound on the expected difference between
\mathbb{E}[D(g(U))]
and its empirical approximation
\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(g(U_{i}))
, for all
g\in\mathcal{G}
and
D\in\mathcal{D}
.

Proposition B.6
.

If
\mathcal{G}\subset\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{K}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})
and
\mathcal{D}\subset\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{1}(B^{p}(0,K),\mathbb{R})
, for i.i.d. random variables
U
and
U_{i}
that are uniform on
[0,1]^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{U_{i}}\Big[\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}\\
D\in\mathcal{D}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{U}[D(g(U)]-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(g(U_{i}))\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\min\limits_{\delta\in[0,1]}\Biggl\{\sqrt{\frac{(\delta+1/n)^{2}\log(n|\mathcal{G}_{1/n}||\mathcal{D}_{1/n}|)}{n}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}(1+\delta^{(1-\frac{d}{2\gamma})}+\log(\delta^{-1})\mathds{1}_{\{2\gamma=d\}})\Biggl\}.

We are first going to use Dudley’s inequality bound (
Vaart and Wellner 2023
, chapter 2). For
g\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{K}
and
D\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{1}
, we have from the Faa di Bruno formula that
D\circ g\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{C}
. Let us take

\Theta=\{D\circ g\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{C}\ |\ D\in\mathcal{D},g\in\mathcal{G}\}

equipped with the metric
M=\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
. For
\theta=D\circ g\in\Theta
, we define the zero-mean sub-Gaussian process (e.g.
(
van Handel 2016
)
, chapter 5) as
X_{\theta}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(g(U_{i}))-\mathbb{E}[D(g(U))]
with
U_{i}\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})
i.i.d. Then as
\sup\limits_{\theta,\theta^{{}^{\prime}}\in\Theta}M(\theta,\theta^{{}^{\prime}})\leq 2
, using Dudley’s bound we obtain
\forall\delta\in[0,2]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}\\
D\in\mathcal{D}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}[D(g(U)]-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(g(U_{i}))\Big]\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G},\ D\in\mathcal{D}\\
\|D\circ g\|_{\infty}\leq\delta\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}[D(g(U))]-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(g(U_{i}))\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{16}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\delta/4}^{2}\sqrt{\log(|\Theta_{\epsilon}|)}d\epsilon.

(B.2)

Let us take care of the first term of (
B.2.1
). Let
\mathcal{G}_{1/n}
and
\mathcal{D}_{1/n}
be minimal
1/n
covering of the class
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{D}
for the distance
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
. Therefore, for any
g\in\mathcal{G}
,
D\in\mathcal{D}
,
\exists g_{n}\in\mathcal{G}_{1/n}
and
D_{n}\in\mathcal{D}_{1/n}
such that
\|g-g_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{n}
and
\|D-D_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{n}
. As
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\gamma}\subset\text{Lip}_{1}
, we have for
\alpha>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G},\ D\in\mathcal{D}\\
\|D\circ g\|_{\infty}\leq\delta\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}[D(g(U))]-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(g(U_{i}))\geq\alpha\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G},\ D\in\mathcal{D}\\
\|D\circ g\|_{\infty}\leq\delta\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}[D_{n}(g_{n}(U))]-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D_{n}(g_{n}(U_{i}))\geq\alpha-\frac{4}{n}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\begin{subarray}{c}\exists\ g_{n}\in\mathcal{G}_{1/n},D_{n}\in\mathcal{D}_{1/n}\\
\|D_{n}\circ g_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq\delta+\frac{2}{n}\end{subarray}\text{ with }\mathbb{E}[D_{n}(g_{n}(U))]-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D_{n}(g_{n}(U_{i}))\geq\alpha-\frac{4}{n}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{n}\in\mathcal{G}_{1/n},D_{n}\in\mathcal{D}_{1/n}\\
\|D_{n}\circ g_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq\delta+\frac{2}{n}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathbb{E}[D_{n}(g_{n}(U))]-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D_{n}(g_{n}(U_{i}))\geq\alpha-\frac{4}{n}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{G}_{1/n}||\mathcal{D}_{1/n}|\exp\left(-\frac{n(\alpha-\frac{4}{n})^{2}}{2(\delta+\frac{2}{n})^{2}}\right),

applying Hoeffding’s inequality to the random variables
Z_{i}=D_{n}(g_{n}(U_{i}))
,
i=1,...,n
. Then for
\tau\in(0,1]
, solving
|\mathcal{G}_{1/n}||\mathcal{D}_{1/n}|\exp\left(-\frac{n(\alpha-\frac{4}{n})^{2}}{2(\delta+\frac{2}{n})^{2}}\right)=\tau
with respect to
\alpha
, we obtain that with probability at least
1-\tau

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G},\ D\in\mathcal{D}\\
\|D\circ g\|_{\infty}\leq\delta\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}[D(g(U))]-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(g(U_{i}))

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sqrt{\frac{(\delta+1/n)^{2}\log(|\mathcal{G}_{1/n}||\mathcal{D}_{1/n}|/\tau)}{n}}+\frac{1}{n}\right).

Taking
\tau=1/n
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G},\ D\in\mathcal{D}\\
\|D\circ g\|_{\infty}\leq\delta\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}[D(g(U))]-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(g(U_{i}))\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sqrt{\frac{(\delta+1/n)^{2}\log(|\mathcal{G}_{1/n}||\mathcal{D}_{1/n}|n)}{n}}+\frac{1}{n}\right).

For the second term of (
B.2.1
), as
\Theta\subset\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{C}
we have
\log(|\Theta_{\epsilon}|)\leq C\log(|(\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{1})_{\epsilon}|)
and from
Donoho et al. 1998
we know that
\log(|(\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{1})_{\epsilon}|)\leq C\epsilon^{-\frac{d}{\gamma}}
so

\displaystyle\frac{16}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\delta/4}^{2}\sqrt{\log(|\Theta_{\epsilon}|)}d\epsilon\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\delta/4}^{2}\epsilon^{-\frac{d}{2\gamma}}d\epsilon

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C\mathds{1}_{\{2\gamma\neq d\}}}{\sqrt{n}(1-\frac{d}{2\gamma})}\left(2^{1-\frac{d}{2\gamma}}-\left(\frac{\delta}{4}\right)^{(1-\frac{d}{2\gamma})}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{C\mathds{1}_{\{2\gamma=d\}}}{\sqrt{n}}(\log(2)-\log(\delta/4)).

Therefore we have

\frac{16}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\delta/4}^{2}\sqrt{\log(|\Theta_{\epsilon}|)}d\epsilon\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}(1+\delta^{(1-\frac{d}{2\gamma})}+\log(4\delta^{-1})\mathds{1}_{\{2\gamma=d\}}).

∎We see that having small classes of functions allows the empirical approximation to be close to the expectation. As we would like our estimator
\hat{g}\in\argmin\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\ \max\limits_{D\in\mathcal{D}}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(X_{i})-D(g(U_{i}))
to obtain a small error
\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}[D(\hat{g}(U))-D(g^{\star}(U))]
for all
D\in\mathcal{D}
, it is then crucial that
\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}\\
D\in\mathcal{D}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}[D(g(U)]-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(g(U_{i}))\Big]
be small. From Proposition
B.6
we can now give the proof of Theorem
3.1
.

Let
\overline{D}_{g}\in\argmax\limits_{D\in\mathcal{D}}L(g,D)
and
\overline{g}\in\argmin\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}}L(g,D^{\star}_{g})
, we can decompose the objective as

\displaystyle L(\hat{g},D^{\star}_{\hat{g}})=

\displaystyle L(\hat{g},D^{\star}_{\hat{g}})-L(\hat{g},\overline{D}_{\hat{g}})+L(\hat{g},\overline{D}_{\hat{g}})-L(\overline{g},D^{\star}_{\overline{g}})+L(\overline{g},D^{\star}_{\overline{g}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathcal{D}}+L(\hat{g},\overline{D}_{\hat{g}})-L_{n}(\hat{g},\overline{D}_{\hat{g}})+L_{n}(\hat{g},\overline{D}_{\hat{g}})-L(\overline{g},\overline{D}_{\overline{g}})

\displaystyle+L(\overline{g},\overline{D}_{\overline{g}})-L(\overline{g},D^{\star}_{\overline{g}})+\Delta_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathcal{D}}+L(\hat{g},\overline{D}_{\hat{g}})-L_{n}(\hat{g},\overline{D}_{\hat{g}})+L_{n}(\hat{g},\hat{D}_{\hat{g}})-L(\overline{g},\overline{D}_{\overline{g}})+\Delta_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathcal{D}}+L(\hat{g},\overline{D}_{\hat{g}})-L_{n}(\hat{g},\overline{D}_{\hat{g}})+L_{n}(\overline{g},\hat{D}_{\overline{g}})-L(\overline{g},\overline{D}_{\overline{g}})+\Delta_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathcal{D}}+L(\hat{g},\overline{D}_{\hat{g}})-L_{n}(\hat{g},\overline{D}_{\hat{g}})+L_{n}(\overline{g},\hat{D}_{\overline{g}})-L(\overline{g},\hat{D}_{\overline{g}})+\Delta_{\mathcal{G}}

using the definition of
\hat{g}
and
\hat{D}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[

\displaystyle L(\hat{g},\overline{D}_{\hat{g}})-L_{n}(\hat{g},\overline{D}_{\hat{g}})\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{D}_{\hat{g}}(\hat{g}(U))-\overline{D}_{\hat{g}}(g^{\star}(U))]-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\overline{D}_{\hat{g}}(\hat{g}(U_{i}))-\overline{D}_{\hat{g}}(g^{\star}(Y_{i}))\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G}\\
D\in\mathcal{D}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}[D(g(U)]-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(g(U_{i}))\Big]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup\limits_{D\in\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{E}[D(g^{\star}(U)]-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(g^{\star}(Y_{i}))\Big].

The first term of the precedent inequality can be controlled by Proposition
B.6
. As
Y_{i}\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})
, doing the same derivation as in the proof of Proposition
B.6
for a fixed
g^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1}
, we can also bound the second term by the same quantity. Then, doing the same thing for
\mathbb{E}\Big[L_{n}(\overline{g},\hat{D}_{\overline{g}})-L(\overline{g},\hat{D}_{\overline{g}})\Big]
, we get the result.
∎

B.2.2
Proof of Proposition
3.2

This result is already well known (Theorem 4.2 in
DeVore et al. 1989
) but we give a new proof in the case
\eta
non integer using wavelet decomposition. The case
\eta
integer can also be proven the same way but at the cost of losing a factor
log(\epsilon)^{2}
coming from the fact that
\mathcal{B}^{\eta}_{\infty,\infty}
does not inject in
\mathcal{H}^{\eta}
but
\mathcal{B}^{\eta,2}_{\infty,\infty}
does.

Let
f\in\mathcal{H}^{\eta}_{1}
and
g\in\mathcal{F}
, as
\mathcal{H}^{0}\xhookrightarrow{}\mathcal{B}^{0}_{\infty,\infty}
we have that there exists
C_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\|f-g\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\geq C_{0}^{-1}\|f-g\|_{\mathcal{B}^{0}_{\infty,\infty}}=C_{0}^{-1}\sup\limits_{j\geq 0}2^{jd/2}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sup\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}|\alpha_{g}(j,l,z)-\alpha_{f}(j,l,z)|.

Therefore,
\forall f\in\mathcal{H}^{\eta}_{1}
, there exists
g\in\mathcal{F}
such that

\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}2^{jd/2}|\alpha_{g}(j,l,z)-\alpha_{f}(j,l,z)|\leq C_{0}\|f-g\|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}\epsilon,

for all
j\geq 0
and
z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}
. On the other hand, as
\mathcal{B}^{\eta}_{\infty,\infty}=\mathcal{H}^{\eta}
, there exists
C_{\eta}>0
such that, if

f
verifies

\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}|\alpha_{f}(j,l,z)|\leq C_{\eta}2^{-j(\eta+d/2)},\ \forall j\geq 0,z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d},

then
f\in\mathcal{H}^{\eta}_{1}
.
Therefore, for all
k\in\{0,...,\lfloor 2^{-j\eta-(d+2)}C_{\eta}(C_{0}\epsilon)^{-1}\rfloor\}^{2^{d}}
, as

\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}k_{l}2^{2-jd/2}C_{0}\epsilon\leq\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}C_{\eta}2^{-j(\eta+d/2)-d}\leq C_{\eta}2^{-j(\eta+d/2)},

then there exists
g\in\mathcal{F}
such that for all
j\geq 0,z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}
,

2^{jd/2}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}|\alpha_{g}(j,l,z)-k_{l}2^{2-jd/2}C_{0}\epsilon|\leq C_{0}\epsilon.

Furthermore, we have

2^{jd/2}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}|\alpha_{g}(j,l,z)-k_{l}2^{2-jd/2}C_{0}\epsilon|\leq C_{0}\epsilon

implies

\ 2^{jd/2}|\alpha_{g}(j,l,z)-k_{l}2^{2-jd/2}C_{0}\epsilon|\leq C_{0}\epsilon,\forall l\in\{1,...,2^{d}\},

which implies

\ 2^{jd/2}|\alpha_{g}(j,l,z)-(k_{l}+1)2^{2-jd/2}C_{0}\epsilon|>C_{0}\epsilon,\forall l\in\{1,...,2^{d}\}.

Then for each sequence

\alpha\in\bigotimes\limits_{j=1}^{\log_{2}(\lfloor(C_{\eta}^{-1}C_{0}\epsilon)^{-\frac{1}{\eta}}\rfloor)-d/\eta}\bigotimes\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\{0,...,\lfloor 2^{-j\eta-(d+2)}C_{\eta}(C_{0}\epsilon)^{-1}\rfloor\}^{2^{d}},

there exists a different
g\in\mathcal{F}
such that

\|g-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\log_{2}(\lfloor(C_{\eta}^{-1}C_{0}\epsilon)^{-\frac{1}{\eta}}\rfloor)}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\alpha_{jlz}C_{0}2^{2-jd/2}\epsilon\ \psi^{per}_{jlk}\|_{\infty}\leq\epsilon.

We stop at the frequency
j=\log_{2}(\lfloor(C_{\eta}^{-1}C_{0}\epsilon)^{-\frac{1}{\eta}}\rfloor)-d/\eta
, as
for
f\in\mathcal{H}^{\eta}_{1}
and
j\geq\log_{2}(\lfloor(C_{\eta}^{-1}C_{0}\epsilon)^{-\frac{1}{\eta}}\rfloor)-d/\eta
we have

2^{jd/2}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}|\alpha_{f}(j,l,z)|\leq 2^{jd/2}C_{\eta}2^{-j(\eta+d/2)}=C_{\eta}2^{d-j\eta}=C_{0}\epsilon,

so
\mathcal{F}
does not have to cover these frequencies.
Then for
\theta\in(0,\frac{C_{0}\epsilon}{3})
, the
\theta
-covering number of
\mathcal{F}
verifies

\displaystyle|\mathcal{F}_{\theta}|

\displaystyle\geq\prod\limits_{j=0}^{\log_{2}(\lfloor(C_{\eta}^{-1}C_{0}\epsilon)^{-\frac{1}{\eta}}\rfloor)-(d+3)/\eta}\prod\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\lfloor 2^{-j\eta-(d+2)}C_{\eta}(C_{0}\epsilon)^{-1}\rfloor^{2^{d}}

\displaystyle\geq\prod\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{\log_{2}(\lfloor(C_{\eta}^{-1}C_{0}\epsilon)^{-\frac{1}{\eta}}\rfloor)-(d+3)/\eta}\}^{d}}2

\displaystyle=\prod\limits_{z\in\{0,...,\lfloor(C_{\eta}^{-1}C_{0}\epsilon)^{-\frac{1}{\eta}}\rfloor 2^{-(d+3)/\eta}\}^{d}}2

\displaystyle\geq C2^{\left(\lfloor(C_{\eta}^{-1}C_{0}\epsilon)^{-\frac{1}{\eta}}\rfloor\right)^{d}}.

We then deduce that

\log(|\mathcal{F}_{\theta}|)\geq C\epsilon^{-\frac{d}{\eta}}.

∎

B.3
Proofs of the results on the properties of
\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\eta,\delta}_{per}
(Section
3.2
)

B.3.1
Proof of Lemma
3.5

Let
g\in\mathcal{B}^{\eta}_{\infty,\infty}(K)
and

D\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{1}
, using a Taylor expansion we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\Big[D(g(U))-D(g^{\star}(U))\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{1}\langle\nabla D(g^{\star}(U)+t(g(U)-g^{\star}(U))),g(U)-g^{\star}(U)\rangle dt\Big]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\partial_{i}D(g^{\star}(U)+t(g(U)-g^{\star}(U)))(g(U)-g^{\star}(U))_{i}\Big]dt.

Let us fix a
t\in[0,1]
and write the functions in their (periodized) wavelets expansions:

D_{i}^{t}(u):=\partial_{i}D(g^{\star}(u)+t(g(u)-g^{\star}(u)))=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\alpha_{D^{t}_{i}}(j,l,z)\psi_{jlz}^{per}(u),

g(u)_{i}=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\alpha_{g}(j,l,w)_{i}\psi_{jlz}^{per}(u),

and

g^{\star}(u)_{i}=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\alpha_{g^{\star}}(j,l,w)_{i}\psi_{jlz}^{per}(u).

As
g-g^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}^{\eta}_{K}\subset\mathcal{B}^{\eta}_{\infty,\infty}(C)
and
D_{i}^{t}\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma-1}_{C}\subset\mathcal{B}^{\gamma-1}_{\infty,\infty}(C)
, we have that for all
i\in\{1,...p\}
and
j\geq 0,

\alpha_{D^{t}_{i}}(j,l,z)(\alpha_{g}(j,l,w)_{i}-\alpha_{g^{\star}}(j,l,w)_{i})\leq C2^{-j(\gamma-1+\eta+d)}.

Then, using the wavelet expansions we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\Big[\partial_{i}D(g^{\star}(U)+t(g(U)-g^{\star}(U)))(g(U)-g^{\star}(U))_{i}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\alpha_{D^{t}_{i}}(j,l,z)(\alpha_{g}(j,l,w)_{i}-\alpha_{g^{\star}}(j,l,w)_{i})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\log_{2}(\lfloor\epsilon^{-1}\rfloor)}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\alpha_{D^{t}_{i}}(j,l,z)(\alpha_{g}(j,l,w)_{i}-\alpha_{g^{\star}}(j,l,w)_{i})

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{j=\log_{2}(\lfloor\epsilon^{-1}\rfloor)+1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}C2^{-j(\gamma-1+\eta+d)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\log_{2}(\lfloor\epsilon^{-1}\rfloor)}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\alpha_{D^{t}_{i}}(j,l,z)(\alpha_{g}(j,l,w)_{i}-\alpha_{g^{\star}}(j,l,w)_{i})+C\epsilon^{\eta+\gamma-1}.

Applying twice the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we get

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\log_{2}(\lfloor\epsilon^{-1}\rfloor)}

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\alpha_{D^{t}_{i}}(j,l,z)(\alpha_{g}(j,l,w)_{i}-\alpha_{g^{\star}}(j,l,w)_{i})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\log_{2}(\lfloor\epsilon^{-1}\rfloor)}\left(\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}|\alpha_{D^{t}_{i}}(j,l,z)|^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}|\alpha_{g}(j,l,w)_{i}-\alpha_{g^{\star}}(j,l,w)_{i}|^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\log_{2}(\lfloor\epsilon^{-1}\rfloor)}2^{2j(\gamma-1)}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}|\alpha_{D^{t}_{i}}(j,l,z)|^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\log_{2}(\lfloor\epsilon^{-1}\rfloor)}2^{-2j(\gamma-1)}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}|\alpha_{g}(j,l,w)_{i}-\alpha_{g^{\star}}(j,l,w)_{i}|^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\log_{2}(\lfloor\epsilon^{-1}\rfloor)}2^{2j(\gamma-1)}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}|C2^{-j(\gamma-1+d/2)}|^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\times\|\Gamma^{-\gamma+1}(g_{i})-\Gamma^{-\gamma+1}(g^{\star}_{i})\|_{\mathcal{B}^{0}_{2,2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\log_{2}(\lfloor\epsilon^{-1}\rfloor)^{1/2}\|\Gamma^{-\gamma+1}(g_{i})-\Gamma^{-\gamma+1}(g^{\star}_{i})\|_{L^{2}},

as
\forall s\geq 0
,
\mathcal{B}^{s}_{2,2}=\mathcal{W}^{s,2}
the
L^{2}
Sobolev space of regularity
s
.
∎

B.3.2
Proof of Proposition
3.6

For
f\in\mathcal{F}_{per}^{\eta,\delta}
we have

f\in\mathcal{H}^{\eta}_{C\log(\delta^{-1})^{2}}
(we do not distinguish between
\eta
integer or not) so using Lemma
3.5
with
\epsilon=\delta
, for any
f^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}^{\eta}_{K}
we have

\displaystyle d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\gamma}}(f_{\#U},f^{\star}_{\#U})

\displaystyle\leq C\log(\delta^{-1})^{C_{2}}\left(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\|\Gamma^{-(\gamma-1)}(f_{i})-\Gamma^{-(\gamma-1)}(f^{\star}_{i})\|_{L^{2}}+C\delta^{\eta-1+\gamma}\right).

Then, for
f
such that
\forall j\leq\log_{2}(\delta^{-1})
and
i\in\{1,...,p\}
,
\alpha_{f_{i}}(j,l,z)=\alpha_{f^{\star}_{i}}(j,l,z)
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Gamma^{-(\gamma-1)}

\displaystyle(f_{i})-\Gamma^{-(\gamma-1)}(f^{\star}_{i})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Gamma^{-(\gamma-1)}(f_{i})-\Gamma^{-(\gamma-1)}(f^{\star}_{i})\|_{\mathcal{B}^{0}_{2,2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=C\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}2^{-2j(\gamma-1)}|\alpha_{f_{i}}(j,l,z)-\alpha_{f^{\star}_{i}}(j,l,z)|^{2}

\displaystyle=C\sum\limits_{j\geq\log_{2}(\delta^{-1})}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}2^{-2j(\gamma-1)}|\alpha_{f^{\star}_{i}}(j,l,z)|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum\limits_{j\geq\log_{2}(\delta^{-1})}^{\infty}2^{-2j(\gamma+\eta-1)}

\displaystyle\leq C2^{-\log_{2}(\delta^{-1})2(\gamma-1+\eta)}=C\delta^{2(\gamma-1+\eta)}.

Let us now bound the covering number for a certain
\epsilon\in(0,1)
. Define

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F}_{per}^{\eta,\delta})_{\epsilon}=\{

\displaystyle f\in\mathcal{F}_{per}^{\eta,\delta}|\ \forall i,j,l,z,\exists k\in[-KC_{\eta}\epsilon^{-1}2^{-j\eta+d}(1+j)^{2},...,KC_{\eta}\epsilon^{-1}2^{-j\eta+d}(1+j)^{2}],

\displaystyle\langle f_{i},\psi_{j,l,z}^{per}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\frac{k\epsilon}{(1+j)^{2}2^{jd/2+d}}\}.

Then for
f\in\mathcal{F}_{per}^{\eta,\delta}
and
f_{\epsilon}\in(\mathcal{F}_{per}^{\eta,\delta})_{\epsilon}
such that

\langle(f_{\epsilon})_{i},\psi_{j,l,z}^{per}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\frac{\epsilon}{(1+j)^{2}2^{jd/2+d}}\lfloor\langle f_{i},\psi_{j,l,z}^{per}\rangle_{L^{2}}\epsilon^{-1}(1+j)^{2}2^{jd/2+d}\rfloor,

we have

\displaystyle\|f_{i}-(f_{\epsilon})_{i}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\|f_{i}-(f_{\epsilon})_{i}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{0,2}_{\infty,\infty}}

\displaystyle=C\sup\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}2^{jd/2}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sup\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}(1+j)^{2}|\alpha_{f_{i}}(j,l,z)-\alpha_{(f_{\epsilon})_{i}}(j,l,z)|

\displaystyle\leq C\sup\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}2^{jd/2}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sup\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}(1+j)^{2}\frac{\epsilon}{(1+j)^{2}2^{jd/2+d}}=C\epsilon

so
(\mathcal{F}_{per}^{\eta,\delta})_{\epsilon}
is an
C\epsilon
covering of
\mathcal{F}_{per}^{\eta,\delta}
. Furthermore

\displaystyle\big|(\mathcal{F}_{per}^{\eta,\delta})_{\epsilon}\big|

\displaystyle=\prod\limits_{j=0}^{\log_{2}(\lfloor\delta^{-1}\rfloor)}\prod\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\prod\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}2^{-j\eta+d+1}(1+j)^{2}C_{\eta}K\epsilon^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq C\prod\limits_{j=0}^{\log_{2}(\lfloor\delta^{-1}\rfloor)}\prod\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\prod\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\epsilon^{-1}=C\prod\limits_{j=0}^{\log_{2}(\lfloor\delta^{-1}\rfloor)}(\epsilon^{-1})^{2^{jd+d}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\epsilon^{-1})^{\log_{2}(\lfloor\delta^{-1}\rfloor)2^{\log_{2}(\lfloor\delta^{-1}\rfloor)d+d}}=C(\epsilon^{-1})^{\log_{2}(\lfloor\delta^{-1}\rfloor)2^{d}\delta^{-d}},

so

\log(\big|(\mathcal{F}_{per}^{\eta,\delta})_{\epsilon}\big|)\leq C\delta^{-d}\log(\lfloor\delta^{-1}\rfloor)\log(\epsilon^{-1}).

∎

B.3.3
Proof of Proposition
3.7

For
g\in\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\eta,\delta}_{per}
and
D\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[D(g(U))-D(g^{\star}(U))]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\langle\int_{0}^{1}\nabla D(g(U)+t(g^{\star}(U)-g(U))dt,g^{\star}(U)-g(U)\rangle]

\displaystyle\leq C\sup\limits_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma-1}_{1}}\mathbb{E}[\langle f(U),g^{\star}(U)-g(U)\rangle].

Then the rest of the proof proceeds as the proof of Proposition
4.3
Section
C.2.2
.

∎

C
Proofs of the bounds of Section
4

C.1
Proof of the bound in the general low dimensional case (Section
4.1
)

In this section, we give the proofs of the results of Section
4
in the order in which they are stated in the paper.

C.1.1
Proof of Theorem
4.1

Using Proposition
3.7
for
\eta=\beta+1
,
\delta=n^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+d}}
and
\epsilon=1/n
we have that

\Delta_{\mathcal{G}}\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-\frac{\beta+\gamma}{2\beta+d}}\ \ \text{and }\ \ \log(|\mathcal{G}_{1/n}|)\leq Cn^{\frac{d}{2\beta+d}}\log(n)^{2}.

Let us now take care of
\Delta_{\mathcal{D}}
. Write
\overline{g}\in\argmin\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}}L(g,D^{\star}_{g})
, then for
g\in\mathcal{G}
, as
D^{\star}_{g,\overline{g}}\in\mathcal{D}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{D\in\mathcal{D}}L(g,D^{\star}_{g})-L(g,D)\leq

\displaystyle L(g,D^{\star}_{g})-L(g,D^{\star}_{g,\overline{g}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[D_{g}^{\star}(g(U))-D_{g}^{\star}(g^{\star}(U))-D^{\star}_{g,\overline{g}}(g(U))+D^{\star}_{g,\overline{g}}(g^{\star}(U))\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[D_{g}^{\star}(g(U))-D_{g}^{\star}(\overline{g}(U))-D^{\star}_{g,\overline{g}}(g(U))+D^{\star}_{g,\overline{g}}(\overline{g}(U))\Big]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\Big[D_{g}^{\star}(\overline{g}(U))-D_{g}^{\star}(g^{\star}(U))-D^{\star}_{g,\overline{g}}(\overline{g}(U))+D^{\star}_{g,\overline{g}}(g^{\star}(U))\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[D_{g}^{\star}(g(U))-D_{g}^{\star}(\overline{g}(U))-D^{\star}_{g,\overline{g}}(g(U))+D^{\star}_{g,\overline{g}}(\overline{g}(U))\Big]+2\Delta_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\Delta_{\mathcal{G}}\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-\frac{\beta+\gamma}{2\beta+d}}.

On the other hand, by definition of
\mathcal{D}
, we have that
|\mathcal{D}_{1/n}|\leq|\mathcal{D}|\leq|\mathcal{G}_{1/n}|^{2}
.

Using Theorem
3.1
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\Big[L(\hat{g},D^{\star}_{\hat{g}})\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\min\limits_{\delta\in[0,1]}\sqrt{\frac{(\delta+1/n)^{2}\log(n|\mathcal{G}_{1/n}||\mathcal{D}_{1/n}|)}{n}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}(1+\delta^{(1-\frac{d}{2\gamma})}+\log(\delta^{-1})\mathds{1}_{\{2\gamma=d\}})

\displaystyle+\Delta_{\mathcal{G}}+\Delta_{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\min\limits_{\delta\in[0,1]}\sqrt{\frac{(\delta+1/n)^{2}n^{\frac{d}{2\beta+d}}}{n}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}(1+\delta^{(1-\frac{d}{2\gamma})}+\log(\delta^{-1})\mathds{1}_{\{2\gamma=d\}})

\displaystyle+\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-\frac{\beta+\gamma}{2\beta+d}}.

Now taking
\delta=n^{-\frac{\gamma}{2\beta+d}}
we have

\sqrt{\frac{(\delta+1/n)^{2}n^{\frac{d}{2\beta+d}}}{n}}\leq 2n^{-\frac{\gamma}{2\beta+d}}n^{\frac{d/2}{2\beta+d}}n^{-\frac{\beta+d/2}{2\beta+d}}=2n^{-\frac{\beta+\gamma}{2\beta+d}}

and

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}(1+\delta^{(1-\frac{d}{2\gamma})})=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}+n^{-\frac{\gamma}{2\beta+d}\frac{\gamma-d/2}{\gamma}-\frac{\beta+d/2}{2\beta+d}}=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}+n^{-\frac{\beta+\gamma}{2\beta+d}},

so we finally obtain

\mathbb{E}\Big[L(\hat{g},D^{\star}_{\hat{g}})\Big]\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\left(n^{-\frac{\beta+\gamma}{2\beta+d}}\vee n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

∎

C.2
Proofs of the bound in the full dimensional case (Section
4.2
)

C.2.1
Proof of Corollary
4.2

Following the proof of Proposition
B.6
, for

\Theta=\{Df\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{C}\ |\ D\in\mathcal{D},f\in\mathcal{F}\}

and

X_{\theta}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(U_{i})f(U_{i})-\int D(x)f(x)d\lambda^{p}(x),

with
U_{i}
i.i.d. uniform random variables on
B^{p}(0,K)
, we get using Dudley’s inequality
\forall\delta\in[0,2K]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in\mathcal{F}\\
D\in\mathcal{D}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\int D(x)f(x)d\lambda^{p}(x)-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(U_{i})f(U_{i})\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in\mathcal{G},\ D\in\mathcal{D}\\
\|Df\|_{\infty}\leq\delta\end{subarray}}\int D(x)f(x)d\lambda^{p}(x)-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(U_{i})f(U_{i})\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{16}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\delta/4}^{2K}\sqrt{\log(|\Theta_{\epsilon}|)}d\epsilon.

Doing as in the proof of Proposition
3.6
, we can show that
\log(|(\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty,\infty})_{\epsilon}|)\leq\epsilon^{-\frac{d}{\gamma}}\log(\epsilon^{-1})
. The rest of the proof follows the same arguments than the end of the proof of Proposition
B.6
and the proof of Theorem
3.1
(see Section
B.2.1
).
∎

C.2.2
Proof of Proposition
4.3

First, let us give the approximation error and covering number of the class
\mathcal{F}^{\eta,\delta}
.

Proposition C.1
.

We have

\Delta_{\mathcal{F}^{\eta,\delta}}:=\sup\limits_{h^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}^{\eta}_{1}}\inf\limits_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{\eta,\delta}}\sup\limits_{D\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}D(x)(f(x)-h^{\star}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)\leq C\delta^{\eta+\gamma},

and
\forall\epsilon\in(0,1)
,

\log(|\mathcal{F}^{\eta,\delta}|_{\epsilon})\leq C\delta^{-p}\log(\lfloor\delta^{-1}\rfloor)\log(\epsilon^{-1}).

Let
h^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}^{\eta}_{1}
and
f\in\mathcal{F}^{\eta,\delta}
be such that for all
j\leq\log_{2}(\delta^{-1})
,
\langle h^{\star},\psi_{j,l,w}\rangle=\langle f,\psi_{j,l,w}\rangle
. Then for
D\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{1}
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}D(x)(f(x)-h^{\star}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}\alpha_{D}(j,l,w)(\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)-\alpha_{h^{\star}}(j,l,w))

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{j\geq log_{2}(\delta^{-1})}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}\alpha_{D}(j,l,w)\alpha_{h^{\star}}(j,l,w)

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{j\geq log_{2}(\delta^{-1})}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}K2^{-j(p+\eta+\gamma)}\mathds{1}_{\{supp(\psi_{jlw})\cap B^{p}(0,K)\neq\varnothing\}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{j\geq log_{2}(\delta^{-1})}^{\infty}C2^{-j(\eta+\gamma)}

\displaystyle=C\delta^{\eta+\gamma}.

For the bound on the covering number see the proof of Proposition
3.6
, Section
B.3.2
.
∎We can now give the proof of Proposition
4.3
.

Let
h^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}^{\eta}_{1}
and define
f\in\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\eta,\delta}
by

f=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\log_{2}(\delta^{-1})}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\{-K2^{j},...,K2^{j}\}^{p}}\alpha_{h^{\star}}(j,l,w)\hat{\psi}_{jlw},

for
\alpha_{h^{\star}}(j,l,w)=\langle h^{\star},\psi_{j,l,w}\rangle
. Then, from Proposition
A.4
there exist coefficients
(\alpha_{f}(j,l,w))
such that

f=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\{-K2^{j},...,K2^{j}\}^{p}}\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)\psi_{jlw},

with
\forall j\leq\log_{2}(\delta^{-1})
,

|\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)-\hat{\alpha}_{f}(j,l,w)|\leq CL^{-1}(2^{-j(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2+p/2)}\delta^{-(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2-\eta)}\log_{2}(\delta^{-1})+2^{-j(\eta+p/2)})

and
\forall j>\log_{2}(\delta^{-1})
,

|\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)|\leq CL^{-1}2^{-j(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2+p/2)}\delta^{-(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2-\eta)}\log_{2}(\delta^{-1}).

As
L\geq\delta^{-(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2+\gamma)}
, we have in particular that

|\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)-\alpha_{h^{\star}}(j,l,w)|\leq C2^{-j(\eta+p/2)}\delta^{\eta+\gamma},\text{ for }j\leq\log_{2}(\delta^{-1})

and

|\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)|\leq C2^{-j(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2+p/2)}\delta^{\eta+\gamma},\text{ for }j>\log_{2}(\delta^{-1}).

Therefore, for
D\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{1}
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}

\displaystyle D(x)(f(x)-h^{\star}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}\alpha_{D}(j,l,w)(\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)-\alpha_{h^{\star}}(j,l,w))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=0}^{log_{2}(\delta^{-1})}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}|\alpha_{D}(j,l,w)(\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)-\alpha_{h^{\star}}(j,l,w))|

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{j>log_{2}(\delta^{-1})}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}|\alpha_{D}(j,l,w)(\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)-\alpha_{h^{\star}}(j,l,w))|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum\limits_{j\leq log_{2}(\delta^{-1})}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}2^{-j(\gamma+p/2)}2^{-j(\eta+p/2)}\delta^{\eta+\gamma}\mathds{1}_{\{supp(\psi_{jlw})\cap B^{p}(0,K)\neq\varnothing\}}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{j>log_{2}(\delta^{-1})}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}|\alpha_{D}(j,l,w)|(|\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)|+|\alpha_{h^{\star}}(j,l,w)|)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta^{\eta+\gamma}+C\sum\limits_{j>log_{2}(\delta^{-1})}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}2^{-j(\gamma+p/2)}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad(2^{-j(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2+p/2)}\delta^{\eta+\gamma}+2^{-j(\eta+p/2)})\mathds{1}_{\{supp(\psi_{jlw})\cap B^{p}(0,K)\neq\varnothing\}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta^{\eta+\gamma}.

Let us now prove the bound on the covering number. Let

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{F}}=\{

\displaystyle f\in\mathcal{B}^{\eta}_{\infty,\infty}(B^{p}(0,K),\mathbb{R},K)|

\displaystyle\ |\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)|\leq CL^{-1}2^{-j(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2+p/2)}\delta^{-(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2-\eta)}\log_{2}(\delta^{-1}),\forall j>log_{2}(\delta^{-1})\}.

Then from Proposition
A.4
, we have that
\forall\epsilon\in(0,1)
,

\log(|(\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\eta,\delta})_{\epsilon}|)\leq C\log(|(\overline{\mathcal{F}})_{\epsilon}|).

Let
\epsilon>L^{-1}\delta^{\eta-1}
and
(\mathcal{F}^{\eta,\delta})_{\epsilon}
an
\epsilon
-covering of
\mathcal{F}^{\eta,\delta}
. For all
f\in\overline{\mathcal{F}}
, writing

\tilde{f}=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{log_{2}(\delta^{-1})}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)\psi_{jlw},

we have
\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{F}^{\eta,\delta}
. Then there exist
f_{\epsilon}\in(\mathcal{F}^{\eta,\delta})_{\epsilon}
such that

\displaystyle\|\tilde{f}-f_{\epsilon}\|_{\infty}\leq\epsilon.

Then,

\displaystyle\|f-f_{\epsilon}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon+\|f-\tilde{f}\|_{\infty}\leq\epsilon+C\|f-\tilde{f}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{0,2}_{\infty,\infty}}

\displaystyle=\epsilon+C\sup\limits_{j\geq log_{2}(\delta^{-1})}(1+j)^{2}2^{jp/2}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sup\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}|\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon+C\sup\limits_{j\geq log_{2}(\delta^{-1})}(1+j)^{2}2^{jp/2}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}L^{-1}2^{-j(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2+p/2)}\delta^{-(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2-\eta)}\log_{2}(\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon+C\log(\delta^{-1})^{C_{2}}L^{-1}\delta^{\eta}.

Therefore we deduce that
(\mathcal{F}^{\eta,\delta})_{\epsilon}
is a
C\epsilon
-covering of
|\overline{\mathcal{F}}|
so

\log(|(\overline{\mathcal{F}})_{\epsilon}|)\leq C\log(|(\mathcal{F}^{\eta,\delta})_{\epsilon}|).

Finally, using Proposition
C.1
we get the result.

∎

C.2.3
Proof of Corollary
4.4

Let
D\in\mathcal{B}^{\gamma,2}_{\infty,\infty}(1)
, then applying Proposition
B.1
for
h_{1}=D
,
h_{2}=f-f^{\star}
,
\tau=\gamma-\alpha
,
t=1
,
r=\frac{\gamma}{\beta+\gamma}
and
q=\frac{\beta+\alpha}{\beta+\gamma}
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}D(x)(f(x)-f^{\star}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\gamma-\alpha}(D)(x)(f(x)-f^{\star}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)\right)^{\frac{\beta+\gamma}{\beta+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\gamma}(D)(x)\Gamma^{\beta}(f-f^{\star})(x)d\lambda^{p}(x)\right)^{\frac{\alpha-\gamma}{\beta+\alpha}}.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}

\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}^{\gamma}(D)(x)\Gamma^{\beta}(f-f^{\star})(x)d\lambda^{p}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}2^{j(\beta+\gamma)}|\alpha_{D}(j,l,w)(\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)-\alpha_{f^{\star}}(j,l,w))|

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}2^{-jp}(1+j)^{-2}C\mathds{1}_{\{supp(\psi_{jlw})\cap B^{p}(0,K)\neq\varnothing\}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{-2}.

∎

C.2.4
Proof of Lemma
4.5

From Proposition
A.4
we have that
\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\beta,n^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+p}}}\subset\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p},\mathbb{R},C)
. Then, for
f\in\mathcal{F},D^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\tilde{\beta}}
and
D\in\mathcal{D}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}

\displaystyle(D^{\star}(x)-D(x))(f(x)-f^{\star}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}(\alpha_{D^{\star}}(j,l,w)-\alpha_{D}(j,l,w))(\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)-\alpha_{f^{\star}}(j,l,w))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}C2^{-jp/2}2^{-j\beta}(\alpha_{D^{\star}}(j,l,w)-\alpha_{D}(j,l,w)).

The map
h:\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
having wavelets coefficients
\alpha_{h}(j,l,w)=2^{-jp/2}
, belongs to
\mathcal{B}^{0}_{\infty,\infty}
which gives the result.
∎

C.2.5
Proof of Proposition
4.6

We bound
\Delta_{\mathcal{D}}
following the proof of Proposition
C.1
. For
f\in\mathcal{F}
and
\overline{D}_{f}\in\mathcal{D}
such that
\langle\overline{D}_{f},\psi_{j,l,w}\rangle=\langle D_{f}^{\star},\psi_{j,l,w}\rangle
for all
j\leq\log_{2}(n^{\frac{1}{2\beta+p}})
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}

\displaystyle(\overline{D}_{f}(x)-D_{f}^{\star}(x))(f(x)-f^{\star}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{j\geq\log_{2}(n^{\frac{1}{2\beta+p}})}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}(\alpha_{D_{f}^{\star}}(j,l,w)(\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)-\alpha_{f^{\star}}(j,l,w))

\displaystyle\leq Cn^{-\frac{\beta+\tilde{\beta}}{2\beta+p}}.

Furthermore, from Proposition
4.3
we have

\Delta_{\mathcal{G}}\leq Cn^{-\frac{\beta+\tilde{\beta}}{2\beta+p}}\text{ and }\log(|\mathcal{G}_{1/n}||\mathcal{D}_{1/n}|)\leq\log(n)^{2}n^{\frac{p}{2\beta+p}}.

Then, we get the result using Corollary
4.2
with
\delta=n^{-\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{2\beta+p}}
.
∎

C.2.6
Proof of Proposition
4.7

If
\beta\geq\tilde{\beta}
and
\gamma\in[\tilde{\beta},\beta]
we have

\mathbb{E}\Big[d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\gamma}}(\hat{f},f^{\star})\Big]\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\tilde{\beta}}}(\hat{f},f^{\star})\Big]\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-\frac{1}{2}},

using Proposition
4.6
. Otherwise, for
D\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}

\displaystyle D(x)(f(x)-f^{\star}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}\alpha_{D}(j,l,w)(\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)-\alpha_{f^{\star}}(j,l,w))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\log(\lfloor n\rfloor)}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}\alpha_{D}(j,l,w)(\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)-\alpha_{f^{\star}}(j,l,w))

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{j=\log(\lfloor n\rfloor)+1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}2^{-j(p+\beta+\gamma)}K\mathds{1}_{\{supp(\psi_{jlw})\cap B^{p}(0,K)\neq\varnothing\}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1+\log(n))^{2}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\log(\lfloor n\rfloor)}(1+j)^{-2}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}|\alpha_{D}(j,l,w)(\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)-\alpha_{f^{\star}}(j,l,w))|

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{j=\log(\lfloor n\rfloor)+1}^{\infty}2^{-j(\beta+\gamma)}C

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1+\log(n))^{2}\sup\limits_{D\in\mathcal{B}^{\gamma,2}_{\infty,\infty}(1)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}D(x)(f(x)-f^{\star}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)+C/n.

Then using Corollary
4.4
with
\alpha=\tilde{\beta}
we have

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{D\in\mathcal{B}^{\gamma,2}_{\infty,\infty}(1)}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}D(x)(f(x)-f^{\star}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)

\displaystyle\leq C\sup\limits_{D\in\mathcal{B}^{\tilde{\beta},2}_{\infty,\infty}(1)}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}D(x)(f(x)-f^{\star}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)\right)^{\frac{\beta+\gamma}{\beta+\tilde{\beta}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}\left(n^{-\frac{\beta+\tilde{\beta}}{2\beta+p}}\right)^{\frac{\beta+\gamma}{\beta+\tilde{\beta}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-\frac{\beta+\gamma}{2\beta+p}},

using Proposition
4.6
.
∎

D
Proof of the interpolation inequality in the manifold case (Section
5.1
)

D.1
Definition of the
(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})
compatibility

We first define the notion of approximate Jacobian (Definition 2.10 in
Federer 1959
).

Definition D.1
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a
d
-dimensional submanifold of
\mathbb{R}^{p}
and a map
T:\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
of regularity
C^{1}
. For
x\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, using the matrix of
\nabla T(x)
with respect to orthonormal bases
of
\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
\mathcal{T}_{T(x)}(\mathcal{M})
, the approximate Jacobian of
T
, noted
\text{ap}_{d}(\nabla T(x))
, is equal to the square root of the sum of the squares
of the determinants of the
d
by
d
minors of this matrix.

We will use multiple times the next proposition.

Proposition D.2
.

(Theorem 3.2.22,
Federer 2014
) For
t>0
,
T:\mathcal{M}^{t}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
of regularity
C^{1}
and
D:\mathcal{M}^{t}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, we have

\int_{\mathcal{M}^{t}}D(x)\text{ap}_{d}(\nabla T(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)=\int_{\mathcal{M}}\int_{T^{-1}(\{z\})}D(x)d\lambda^{p-d}(x)d\lambda_{\mathcal{M}}(z),

for
\lambda_{\mathcal{M}}
the volume measure on the submanifold
\mathcal{M}
.

Writing
\mathcal{M}_{g}:=g(\mathbb{T}^{d})
for the image of the torus by a map
g
, we can now define the notion of
(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})
compatibility.

Definition D.3
.

For
g,g^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{K}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})
and
t\in(0,r_{g^{\star}}/2)
such that

\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{M}_{g},\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}})<t
. We say that a map
T:\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}^{t}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
is
(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})_{K_{T}}
-compatible with a radius
t
if:

i)

T\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{K_{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}^{t},\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}})
and the restriction
T_{|_{\mathcal{M}_{g}}}:\mathcal{M}_{g}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
is a diffeomorphism such that
(T\circ g)_{\#U}
admits a density
f_{T}\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta}_{K_{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}},\mathbb{R})
with respect to the volume measure on
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
.

ii)

For all
x\in\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
,

T^{-1}(\{x\})-x\subset E_{x}
a
p-d
dimensional subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{p}
and

(T^{-1}(\{x\})-x)\cap B^{p}(x,t)=B_{E_{x}}(0,t).

iii)

For any function
f\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}^{t},\mathbb{R})
, the map
x\mapsto\int_{T^{-1}(\{x\})}f(y)d\lambda^{p-d}_{E_{x}}(y)
belongs to
\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{K_{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}},\mathbb{R})
.

iv)

For
T_{g}^{-1}:\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{g}
the inverse application such that
T_{g}^{-1}\circ T_{|_{\mathcal{M}_{g}}}=\text{Id}_{|_{\mathcal{M}_{g}}}
,
T_{g}^{-1}\circ T\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{K_{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}^{t},\mathcal{M}_{g})
. Furthermore, the approximate Jacobian of
T
(see Definition
D.1
)
x\mapsto\text{ap}_{d}(\nabla T(x))
is bounded below by
K_{T}^{-1}
.

Requiring this compatibility seems to be quite a strong property but we will show in Proposition
D.5
that actually, as long as the
d_{\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1}}
distance between the measures is relatively small, there always exists a
(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})
-compatible map.

The idea behind Definition
D.3
is that we would like to project
\mathcal{M}_{g}
onto
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
with a
C^{\beta+1}
-diffeomorphism. From
(
Leobacher and Steinicke 2018
)
, we know that the orthogonal projection on a manifold of regularity
\beta+1
is only of regularity
\beta
so unfortunately, we cannot use it here. Therefore, we define the notion of
(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})
-compatible map as a "pseudo" projection of regularity
\beta+1
. This notion regroup technical properties we will need in the proof of Theorem
5.1
. They are essentially properties that the orthogonal projection onto
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
would verify if
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
was of regularity
\beta+2
and
\mathcal{M}_{g}
,
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
are close enough. Indeed, to have the notion of compatibility, we will need the sets
\mathcal{M}_{g}
and
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
to be close in Hausdorff distance. First, let us show that the Hausdorff distance between the image of
g
and
g^{\star}
is controlled by the
d_{\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1}}
norm.

Proposition D.4
.

Let
g,g^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{K}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})
, then for all
t>0
, if

\sup\limits_{D\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}[D(g(U))-D(g^{\star}(U))]\leq t^{(d+1)(2\beta+1)},

then

W_{1}(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\leq Ct^{d+1}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{M}_{g},\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}})<Ct.

Suppose first that
\beta
is not an integer. Then,
using Corollary
B.2
with
\theta=1
,
\theta_{1}=1/2
and
\theta_{2}=\beta+1
, we have

\displaystyle W_{1}

\displaystyle(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})

\displaystyle\leq 2K\sup\limits_{D\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}[D(g(U))-D(g^{\star}(U))]

\displaystyle\leq C\sup\limits_{D\in\mathcal{H}^{1/2}_{1}}\left(\mathbb{E}[D(g(U))-D(g^{\star}(U))]\right)^{\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}}\sup\limits_{D\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1}}\left(\mathbb{E}[D(g(U))-D(g^{\star}(U))]\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta+1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sup\limits_{D\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1}}\left(\mathbb{E}[D(g(U))-D(g^{\star}(U))]\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta+1}}.

If now
\beta
is an integer, then using Corollary
B.2
with
\theta=1
,
\theta_{1}=\frac{\beta-1/2}{2\beta}
and
\theta_{2}=\beta+3/2
, we get the same result.
Then by hypothesis we obtain

\displaystyle W_{1}(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{d+1}.

Suppose that
\max_{x\in\mathcal{M}_{g}}d(x,\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}})\geq\delta
. For
u\in[0,1]^{d}
such that
d(g(u),\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}})\geq\delta
, using the dual formulation of the
1
-Wasserstein distance, we have that

\displaystyle W_{1}(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})

\displaystyle=\sup\limits_{D\in\text{Lip}_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}[D(g(U))-D(g^{\star}(U))]

\displaystyle\geq\int_{[0,1]^{d}}d(g(z),\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}})d\lambda^{d}(z)>\int_{B^{d}(u,\frac{\delta}{2K})}\frac{\delta}{2}d\lambda^{d}(z)\geq C\delta^{d+1}.

Therefore we can deduce that

\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{M}_{g},\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}})\leq Ct.

∎In this proposition, the exponents are not optimal but are sufficient for our purpose. Using this result, let show that if the distance
d_{\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1}}(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})
is small enough, then there exists a
(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})
-compatible map.

Proposition D.5
.

Let
g,g^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{K}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})
that verify the
K
-manifold regularity condition with
g^{\star}
that verifies the
K
-density regularity condition. There exist constants
C,C_{2}>0
such that if

\sup\limits_{D\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}[D(g(U))-D(g^{\star}(U))]\leq C^{-1},

then
g
verifies the
C_{2}
-density
regularity condition and there exists a
(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})_{K_{T}}
-compatible map with radius
t\geq C_{2}^{-1}
and
K_{T}\leq C_{2}
.

The proof of Proposition
D.5
can be found in the following Section.

D.2
Proof of Proposition
D.5

The proof of proposition
D.5
breaks down as follow:
we first approximate
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
by a manifold
\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}
of regularity
\beta+2
using convolution and show that its reach is bounded below. Using that
g_{\#U}
is close to
g^{\star}_{\#U}
for the
d_{\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1}}
distance, we show that
g
is a local diffeomorphism. We then show that
\pi_{s|_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}}:\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}
the restriction to
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
of
\pi_{s}
the orthogonal projection onto
\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}
, is a
C^{\beta+1}
diffeomorphism. Finally we define our map T as
T(x)=(\pi_{s|_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}})^{-1}\circ\pi_{s}(x)
and show that it verifies all the conditions to be
(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})
-compatible.

D.2.1
Approximation of
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
by a manifold
\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}
of regularity
\beta+2

Let
s\in(0,r_{g^{\star}}/2)
and
R_{s}:\mathbb{T}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
defined by
R_{s}(u)=0\vee(1-\|u\|/s)/C_{s}
with
C_{s}
such that
\|R_{s}\|_{L^{1}}=1
. Then define
g_{s}:\mathbb{T}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{p}
by

g_{s}:=g^{\star}\ast R_{s}.

We have that
g_{s}
belongs to
\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{K}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})\cap\mathcal{H}^{\beta+2}_{Ks^{-1}}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})
, verifies that

\|g^{\star}-g_{s}\|_{\infty}\leq Ks

and
\|g^{\star}-g_{s}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}\leq Cs
. Write
C_{0}
for the smallest
C>0
such that

\|g^{\star}-g_{s}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}\leq C_{0}s.

We do not fix the value of
s
yet because we will lower it all along the proof to suit our needs. Let us show that
g_{s}
is a diffeomorphism.

Proposition D.6
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that if
s\in(0,C^{-1})
, the map
g_{s}
is a diffeomorphism on its image.

For
u\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
and
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\|\nabla g_{s}(u)\frac{w}{\|w\|}\|\geq\|\nabla g^{\star}(u)\frac{w}{\|w\|}\|-C_{0}s\geq K^{-1}-C_{0}s\geq\frac{1}{2K}

for
s\leq(4KC_{0})^{-1}
, so
g_{s}
is a local diffeomorphism. Suppose there exists
u,v\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
, such that
g_{s}(u)=g_{s}(v)
and
u\neq v
. Then as

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle g_{s}(u)-g_{s}(v)\|

\displaystyle=\|\nabla g_{s}(u)(v-u)+\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}g_{s}(u+t(v-u))(v-u)^{2}(1-t)dt\|

\displaystyle\geq\|\nabla g^{\star}(u)(v-u)\|-\|\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}g^{\star}(u+t(v-u))(v-u)^{2}(1-t)dt\|-2C_{0}s\|u-v\|

\displaystyle\geq K^{-1}\|u-v\|-\frac{K}{2}\|u-v\|^{2}-2C_{0}s\|u-v\|

\displaystyle=\frac{K}{2}\|u-v\|(2K^{-2}-4\frac{C_{0}}{K}s-\|u-v\|),

we deduce that
\|u-v\|\geq 2K^{-2}-4\frac{C_{0}}{K}s
. Then for
s\leq(16C_{0}K^{3})^{-1}
, we have

\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|\leq\|g_{s}(u)-g_{s}(v)\|+2C_{0}s\leq(8K^{3})^{-1}.

As
\|u-v\|\geq K^{-2}
, applying Lemma
D.19
, we obtain that
r_{g^{\star}}\leq(16K^{3})^{-1}
which is impossible as
g^{\star}
verifies the
K
-manifold regularity condition. Therefore,
g_{s}
is injective and so is a diffeomorphism.
∎

Let us show that the submanifold
\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}=g_{s}([0,1]^{d})
as a stricly positive reach.

Lemma D.7
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that if
s\in(0,C^{-1})
, we have that the reach
r_{g_{s}}
of
\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}
, verifies

r_{g_{s}}\geq C^{-1}.

Let
u,v\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
, recall from the proof of proposition
D.6
that for
s\leq(4KC_{0})^{-1}
, we have
\|\nabla g_{s}(v)(u-v)\|\geq\frac{\|u-v\|}{2K}
. We are going to use characterization of the reach for submanifolds of Lemma
D.18
. Using a Taylor expansions on
g_{s}
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\|g_{s}(u)-g_{s}(v)\|^{2}}{2d(g_{s}(u)-g_{s}(v),\mathcal{T}_{g_{s}(v)}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}))}=\frac{\|\nabla g_{s}(v)(u-v)+O(\|u-v\|^{2})\|^{2}}{2d(\nabla g_{s}(v)(u-v)+O(\|u-v\|^{2}),\mathcal{T}_{g_{s}(v)}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}))}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\|\nabla g_{s}(v)(u-v)\|^{2}+2\langle\nabla g_{s}(v)(u-v),O(\|u-v\|^{2})\rangle+\|O(\|u-v\|^{2})\|^{2}}{2d(\nabla g_{s}(v)(u-v),\mathcal{T}_{g_{s}(v)}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}))+2d(O(\|u-v\|^{2})\|,\mathcal{T}_{g_{s}(v)}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}))}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\frac{K^{-2}}{4}\|u-v\|^{2}-2K^{2}\|u-v\|^{3}-K^{2}\|u-v\|^{4}}{2K\|u-v\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{8K^{3}}(1-K^{4}(8\|u-v\|+4\|u-v\|^{2})),

(D.1)

where we used that
d(\nabla g_{s}(v)(u-v),\mathcal{T}_{g_{s}(v)}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}))=0
. Then
\|u-v\|\leq(32K^{4})^{-1}
implies that

\frac{\|g_{s}(u)-g_{s}(v)\|^{2}}{2d(g_{s}(u)-g_{s}(v),\mathcal{T}_{g_{s}(v)}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}))}>(16K^{3})^{-1}.

Therefore, if
r_{g_{s}}\leq(16K^{3})^{-1}
we have

r_{g_{s}}=\min\limits_{\|u-v\|\geq(32K^{4})^{-1}}\frac{\|g_{s}(u)-g_{s}(v)\|^{2}}{2d(g_{s}(u)-g_{s}(v),\mathcal{T}_{g_{s}(v)}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}))}.

Let

(u^{\star},v^{\star})\in\argmin\limits_{\|u-v\|\geq(32K^{4})^{-1}}\frac{\|g_{s}(u)-g_{s}(v)\|^{2}}{2d(g_{s}(u)-g_{s}(v),\mathcal{T}_{g_{s}(v)}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}))},

then

\displaystyle\|g^{\star}(u^{\star})-g^{\star}(v^{\star})\|

\displaystyle\leq\|g_{s}(u^{\star})-g_{s}(v^{\star})\|+2C_{0}s

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|g_{s}(u^{\star})-g_{s}(v^{\star})\|^{2}}{d(g_{s}(u^{\star})-g_{s}(v^{\star}),\mathcal{T}_{g_{s}(v^{\star})}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}))}+2C_{0}s

\displaystyle=2r_{g_{s}}+2C_{0}s.

Then, if
r_{g_{s}}\vee C_{0}s<(2^{8}K^{5})^{-1}
,
we have
\|g^{\star}(u^{\star})-g^{\star}(v^{\star})\|<(2^{6}K^{5})^{-1}
and as
\|u^{\star}-v^{\star}\|\geq(32K^{4})^{-1}
, applying
Lemma
D.19
, we obtain
r_{g^{\star}}\leq(2^{7}K^{5})^{-1}

which is impossible as
r_{g^{\star}}\geq K^{-1}
. Therefore, taking
s<(C_{0}2^{8}K^{5})^{-1}
we have
r_{g_{s}}\geq(2^{8}K^{5})^{-1}
.
∎

D.2.2
Properties of the projection onto
\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}

Define
\pi_{s}:\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}^{r_{g_{s}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}
as the orthogonal projection.
Let us first show that
\pi_{s}
verifies the point
ii)
of Definition
D.3
on a neighborhood of
\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}
.

Proposition D.8
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that the projection
\pi_{s}
verifies the point
ii)
of Definition
D.3
on
\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}^{C^{-1}}
.

Let
t\in(0,r_{gs}/2)
and
x\in\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}^{t}
,
h\in\mathcal{T}_{\pi_{s}(x)}(M_{g_{s}})^{\perp}
with
\|h\|\leq t
,
u=g_{s}^{-1}\circ\pi_{s}(x)
and
v\in[0,1]^{d}
. Define
\gamma:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{T}^{d}
by

\gamma(r)=g_{s}^{-1}\circ\pi_{s}(x+rh).

Then, we have that
\gamma
is
4Kh
-Lipschitz as
\pi_{s}
is
2
-Lipschitz on
\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}^{t}
(see formula (
D.3
) and equation (
D.4
)) and
g_{s}^{-1}
is
2K
-Lipschitz. We would like to show that
\pi_{s}(x+h)=\pi_{s}(x)
i.e

\|x+h-g_{s}(u+v)\|>\|x+h-g_{s}(u)\|.

Then as
\gamma
is
4Kh
-Lipschitz it is sufficient to prove it for
v\in B^{d}(0,4Kh)
. We have

\displaystyle\|x+h-g_{s}(u+v)\|^{2}=

\displaystyle\|x+h-g_{s}(u)\|^{2}+2\langle x+h-g_{s}(u),-\nabla g_{s}(u)v+O(\|v\|^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle+\|-\nabla g_{s}(u)v+O(\|v\|^{2})\|^{2}.

On the ohter hand,
x+h-g_{s}(u)=x+h-\pi_{s}(x)\in\text{Im}(\nabla g_{s}(u))^{\perp}
the orthogonal of the image of
\nabla g_{s}(u)
, so

\displaystyle\|x+h-g_{s}(u+v)\|^{2}=

\displaystyle\|x+h-g_{s}(u)\|^{2}+2\langle x+h-g_{s}(u),O(\|v\|^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle+\|\nabla g_{s}(u)v\|^{2}-2\langle\nabla g_{s}(u)v,O(\|v\|^{2})\rangle+O(\|v\|^{4})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\|x+h-g_{s}(u)\|^{2}-2\|x+h-g_{s}(u)\|K\|v\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{K^{-2}}{4}\|v\|^{2}-2K\|v\|K\|v\|^{2}-K^{2}\|v\|^{4}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\|x+h-g_{s}(u)\|^{2}+\|v\|^{2}(\frac{K^{-2}}{4}-4tK-2K^{2}\|v\|-K^{2}\|v\|^{2})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\|x+h-g_{s}(u)\|^{2}+\|v\|^{2}(\frac{K^{-2}}{4}-K^{3}(8t+4Kt^{2}))

where we used that
\|v\|\leq 4Kh\leq 4Kt
.
Then for
t=K^{-5}/2^{6}
we have
\|x+h-g(u+v)\|>\|x+h-g(u)\|
, so
\pi_{s}
verifies
ii)
for a radius
t
.
∎

Lemma D.9
.

The map
\pi_{s|_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}}:\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}
, i.e. the restriction of the projection
\pi_{s}
to the manifold
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
, is injective.

Suppose there exist
u,v\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
different such that
\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(u))=\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(v))
. We have

\displaystyle\|g_{s}(u)-g_{s}(v)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|+2C_{0}s

\displaystyle\leq\|g^{\star}(u)-\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(u))\|+\|g^{\star}(v)-\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(u))\|+2C_{0}s\leq 4C_{0}s

Suppose first that
\|u-v\|\geq(32K^{4})^{-1}
and let

(x,y)\in\argmin\limits_{\|a-b\|\geq(32K^{4})^{-1}}\|g_{s}(a)-g_{s}(b)\|.

As detailed in the proof of Lemma
D.7
, we have that
g_{s}(x)-g_{s}(y)\in\mathcal{T}_{g_{s}(y)}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}})^{\perp}
and

\displaystyle r_{g_{s}}

\displaystyle=\min\limits_{\|a-b\|\geq(32K^{4})^{-1}}\frac{\|g_{s}(a)-g_{s}(b)\|^{2}}{2d(g_{s}(a)-g_{s}(b),\mathcal{T}_{g_{s}(b)}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}))}\leq\frac{\|g_{s}(x)-g_{s}(y)\|^{2}}{2d(g_{s}(x)-g_{s}(y),\mathcal{T}_{g_{s}(y)}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}))}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\|g_{s}(x)-g_{s}(y)\|\leq\frac{1}{2}\|g_{s}(u)-g_{s}(v)\|\leq 2C_{0}s.

As from Lemma
D.7

we have
r_{g_{s}}\geq C^{-1}
, supposing
s>0
is small enough, this is impossible and so
\|u-v\|\leq(32K^{4})^{-1}
.

Let
F:[0,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{p}
defined by
F(t)=g^{\star}(u+t(u-v))

then

\displaystyle|d(F(t)

\displaystyle,\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}})-d(g^{\star}(u)+t(g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)),\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}})|

\displaystyle\leq\|F(t)-(g^{\star}(u)+t(g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)))\|

\displaystyle\leq t\|\nabla g^{\star}(u)(u-v)-(g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v))\|+K\|u-v\|^{2}t^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Kt\|u-v\|^{2}+K\|u-v\|^{2}t^{2}=K\|u-v\|^{2}(t+t^{2}).

(D.2)

We have

\displaystyle\langle

\displaystyle g^{\star}(v)-\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(v)),g^{\star}(v)-g^{\star}(u)\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle g^{\star}(v)-g^{\star}(u)+g^{\star}(u)-\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(u)),g^{\star}(v)-g^{\star}(u)\rangle

\displaystyle=\|g^{\star}(v)-g^{\star}(u)\|^{2}-\langle g^{\star}(u)-\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(u)),g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\rangle,

so without loss of generality we can suppose that
\langle g^{\star}(u)-\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(u)),g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\rangle\geq 0
(otherwise we would have

\langle g^{\star}(v)-\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(v)),g^{\star}(v)-g^{\star}(u)\rangle\geq 0
). Let
C_{1}>0
be the constant from Proposition
D.8
such that
\pi_{s}
verifies the point
ii)
of Definition
D.3
on
\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}^{C_{1}^{-1}}
. Supposing
s\leq(2C_{0}C_{1})^{-1}
we have
\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}},\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}})\leq C_{0}s<C_{1}^{-1}/2
, then for
t\in[0,\frac{C_{1}^{-1}}{2\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|}]
, using Proposition
D.8
we have
\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(u)+t(g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)))=\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(u))
. Then

\displaystyle d(g^{\star}(u)+t(g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)),\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}})^{2}=\|g^{\star}(u)+t(g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v))-\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(u))\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|g^{\star}(u)-\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(u))\|^{2}+2t\langle g^{\star}(u)-\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(u)),g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\rangle+t^{2}\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq t^{2}\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|^{2}.

Using (
D.2.2
) for
t=\frac{2C_{0}s}{\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|}
, we have

\displaystyle d(F(\frac{2C_{0}s}{\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|}),\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}})\geq

\displaystyle d(g^{\star}(u)+\frac{2C_{0}s}{\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|}(g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)),\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}})

\displaystyle-K\|u-v\|^{2}\left(\frac{2C_{0}s}{\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|}+\frac{(2C_{0}s)^{2}}{\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|^{2}}\right).

Then as
\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|\leq\|g^{\star}(u)-\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(u))\|+\|g^{\star}(v)-\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(v))\|\leq 2C_{0}s
, we have
\frac{2C_{0}s}{\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|}\leq\left(\frac{2C_{0}s}{\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|}\right)^{2}
so

\displaystyle d(F(\frac{2C_{0}s}{\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|}),\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}})\geq

\displaystyle\frac{2C_{0}s}{\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|}\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|

\displaystyle-2K\|u-v\|^{2}\left(\frac{2C_{0}s}{\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2C_{0}s-8KC_{0}^{2}s^{2}\left(\frac{\|u-v\|}{\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2C_{0}s(1-4KC_{0}s\left(\frac{\|u-v\|}{K^{-1}\|u-v\|-K\|u-v\|^{2}}\right)^{2})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2C_{0}s(1-16K^{3}C_{0}s)

where we used that
\|u-v\|\leq(32K^{4})^{-1}
implies that
\|u-v\|-K^{2}\|u-v\|^{2}\geq\|u-v\|/2
.
Then taking
s\leq(2^{6}K^{3}C_{0})^{-1}
we have
d(F(\frac{2C_{0}s}{\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|},\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}})>C_{0}s
which is impossible by definition of
g_{s}
.
∎

From
(
Leobacher and Steinicke 2018
)
we know that the canonical projection
\pi
onto a submanifold
\mathcal{M}
with strictly positive reach
r
, verifies

\nabla\pi(x)=\left(\text{Id}_{\mathcal{T}_{\pi(x)}(\mathcal{M})}-\|x-\pi(x)\|L_{\pi(x),v}\right)^{-1}P_{\mathcal{T}_{\pi(x)}(\mathcal{M})},

(D.3)

for
L_{\pi(x),v}
the shape operator in the direction
v=\frac{x-\pi(x)}{\|x-\pi(x)\|}
. From Corollary 3 in
Leobacher and Steinicke 2018
, we also have that

\|\left(\text{Id}_{\mathcal{T}_{\pi(x)}(\mathcal{M})}-\|x-\pi(x)\|L_{\pi(x),v}\right)^{-1}\|\leq(1-\|x-\pi(x)\|/r)^{-1}.

(D.4)

Furthermore if
\mathcal{M}
is of regularity
\beta+2
, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on the
\mathcal{H}^{\beta+2}
norm of the charts such that

\|\pi_{|_{\mathcal{M}^{r/2}}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}}\leq C

(D.5)

Using these results, we obtain in the next proposition that the restriction of

\pi_{s}
to the manifold
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
is a diffeomorphism.

Proposition D.10
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that if
s\leq C^{-1}
, then
the map
\pi_{s|_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}}
is a diffeomorphism.

From (
D.5
) we have

\|\pi_{s|_{\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}^{r_{g_{s}}/2}}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}}\leq Cs^{-1}.

(D.6)

To show that
\pi_{s|_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}}
is a local diffeomorphism, let us show that if there existed
u
such that
\lambda_{\min}(\nabla(\pi_{s}\circ g^{\star})(u)^{\top}\nabla(\pi_{s}\circ g^{\star})(u))
was too small, then the Hausdorff distance between
\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}
and
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
would be too big. Let
u\in[0,1]^{d}
and
h>0
, then for
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
\|v\|=1
and

\langle\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(u))-g^{\star}(u),\nabla(\pi_{s}\circ g^{\star}-g^{\star})(u)v\rangle\geq 0,

we have

\displaystyle\|\pi_{s}

\displaystyle(g^{\star}(u+hv))-g^{\star}(u+hv)\|\geq\|\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(u))-g^{\star}(u)+\nabla(\pi_{s}\circ g^{\star}-g^{\star})(u)hv\|-2Kh^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(\|\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(u))-g^{\star}(u)\|^{2}+2\langle\pi_{s}(g^{\star}(u))-g^{\star}(u),\nabla(\pi_{s}\circ g^{\star}-g^{\star})(u)hv\rangle

\displaystyle+\|\nabla(\pi_{s}\circ g^{\star}-g^{\star})(u)hv\|^{2}\big)^{1/2}-2Kh^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle h\|\nabla(\pi_{s}\circ g^{\star}-g^{\star})(u)v\|-2Kh^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle h(\|\nabla g^{\star}(u)v\|-\|\nabla(\pi_{s}\circ g^{\star})(u)v\|)-2Kh^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle h(K^{-1}-\|\nabla(\pi_{s}\circ g^{\star})(u)v\|)-2Kh^{2}.

As
\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}},\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}})\leq Ks
we deduce with
h=(K^{-1}-\|\nabla(\pi_{s}\circ g^{\star})(u)v\|)/4K
that

\frac{(K^{-1}-\|\nabla(\pi_{s}\circ g^{\star})(u)v\|)^{2}}{8K}\leq Ks

so

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}(\nabla(\pi_{s}\circ g^{\star})(u)^{\top}\nabla(\pi_{s}\circ g^{\star})(u))^{1/2}\geq K^{-1}-\sqrt{8s}K.

(D.7)

In particular
\pi_{s}\circ g^{\star}
is an immersion and therefore
\pi_{s|_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}}:\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}
is a local diffeomorphism. As
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
is compact without boundary,
\pi_{s}(\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}})
is a compact boundaryless submanifold of
\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}
so being of same dimension, there are equal. We can conclude that
\pi_{s|_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}}
is a diffeomorphism.
∎

D.2.3
Density regularity of
g

As for all
u\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
we have
\lambda_{\min}((\nabla g^{\star}(u))^{\top}\nabla g^{\star}(u))^{1/2}\geq 1/K
, let us show that if
\inf\limits_{u\in[0,1]^{d}}\lambda_{\min}((\nabla g(u))^{\top}\nabla g(u))
was to small, then the distane
d_{\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1}}(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})
would be too big.

Proposition D.11
.

There exist constants
C,\delta>0
such that for all
\tau>\delta
, if

\inf\limits_{u\in\mathbb{T}^{d}}\inf\limits_{w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\nabla g(u)\frac{w}{\|w\|}\|\leq K^{-\tau},

then

d_{\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1}}(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\geq CK^{-\delta(d+1)(2\beta+1)}.

Let
\tau>3
and suppose that there exists
u_{0}\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
and
w_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\|w_{0}\|=1
such that
\|\nabla g(u_{0})w_{0}\|\leq K^{-\tau}
. For
\delta\in(3,\tau)
, define
D_{\delta}:\mathcal{M}^{K^{-2}/2}_{g^{\star}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

D_{\delta}(x)=(\frac{K^{-\delta}}{8}\wedge(\frac{K^{-\delta}}{4}-\|\pi(x)-\pi(g(u_{0}))\|))\vee 0

for
\pi
the canonical projection onto
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
. As
D_{\delta}
is
2
-Lipschitz (see (
D.3
)), we have

\displaystyle W_{1}(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[D_{\delta}(g(U))-D_{\delta}(g^{\star}(U))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[D_{\delta}(g(U))\mathds{1}_{\{\|\pi(g(U))-\pi(g(u_{0}))\|\leq\frac{K^{-\delta}}{4}\}}]

(D.8)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[D_{\delta}(g^{\star}(U))\mathds{1}_{\{\|\pi(g^{\star}(U))-\pi(g(u_{0}))\|\leq\frac{K^{-\delta}}{4}\}}]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[\frac{K^{-\delta}}{8}\mathds{1}_{\{\|\pi(g(U))-\pi(g(u_{0}))\|\leq\frac{K^{-\delta}}{8}\}}-\frac{K^{-\delta}}{4}\mathds{1}_{\{\|\pi(g^{\star}(U))-\pi(g(u_{0}))\|\leq\frac{K^{-\delta}}{4}\}}]

(D.9)

Let us first bound below the quantity
\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\{\|\pi(g(U))-\pi(g(u_{0}))\|\leq\frac{K^{-\delta}}{8}\}}]
. Let
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and write
v_{0}=\langle v,w_{0}\rangle w_{0}
,
v_{0}^{\perp}=v-v_{0}
. Using a Taylor expansion on the path
\zeta:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
defined by

\zeta(t)=u_{0}+4(\frac{t}{2}\wedge\frac{1}{2})v_{0}+4((\frac{t}{2}-\frac{1}{2})\vee 0)v^{\perp}_{0},

we have

\displaystyle\|g(u_{0}+v)-g(u_{0})\|

\displaystyle=\|\int_{0}^{1}\nabla g(\zeta(t))\dot{\zeta}(t)dt\|

\displaystyle=\|\int_{0}^{1}\left(\nabla g(u_{0}+tv_{0})v_{0}+\nabla g(u_{0}+v_{0}+tv_{0}^{\perp})v^{\perp}_{0}\right)dt\|

\displaystyle=\|\int_{0}^{1}\left(\nabla g(u_{0})v_{0}+\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}g(u_{0}+stv_{0})tv_{0}^{2})ds+\nabla g(u_{0}+v_{0}+tv_{0}^{\perp})v^{\perp}_{0}\right)dt\|

\displaystyle\leq K^{-\tau}\|v_{0}\|+\frac{K}{2}\|v_{0}\|^{2}+K\|v_{0}^{\perp}\|.

Therefore, if
\|v_{0}\|\leq K^{-\frac{\delta+1}{2}}/2^{6}
and
\|v_{0}^{\perp}\|\leq K^{-(\delta+1)}/2^{5}
then

\|\pi(g(u_{0}+v))-\pi(g(u_{0}))\|\leq 2\|g(u_{0}+v)-g(u_{0})\|\leq\frac{K^{-\delta}}{8}.

Writing
C_{d-1}
the volume of the
d-1
-dimensional ball, we can conclude that

\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\{\|\pi(g(U))-\pi(g(u_{0}))\|\leq\frac{K^{-\delta}}{8}\}}]\geq C_{d-1}\left(\frac{K^{-(\delta+1)}}{2^{5}}\right)^{d-1}\frac{K^{-\frac{\delta+1}{2}}}{2^{6}}.

Let us now bound the quantity
\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\{\|\pi(g^{\star}(U))-\pi(g(u_{0}))\|\leq\frac{K^{-\delta}}{4}\}}]
. For
u\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\pi(g^{\star}(u))-\pi\circ g(u_{0})\|=

\displaystyle\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}\circ g^{\star-1}\circ\pi\circ g(u_{0})\|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\|\nabla g^{\star}(g^{\star-1}\circ\pi\circ g(u_{0}))(u-g^{\star-1}\circ\pi\circ g(v_{0}))\|

\displaystyle-K(\|u-g^{\star-1}\circ\pi\circ g(u_{0})\|^{2})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle K^{-1}\|u-g^{\star-1}\circ\pi\circ g(u_{0})\|-K\|u-g^{\star-1}\circ\pi\circ g(u_{0})\|^{2}.

(D.10)

As
r_{g^{\star}}\geq K^{-1}
, from Lemma
D.19
we have

\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|\leq K^{-3}/4\ \text{ imply }\ \|u-v\|\leq K^{-2}/2,

then,

\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}\circ g^{\star-1}\circ\pi\circ g(u_{0})\|\leq\frac{K^{-\delta}}{4}\ \text{ imply }\ \|u-g^{\star-1}\circ\pi\circ g(u_{0})\|\leq K^{-2}/2.

(D.11)

Solving
K^{-1}x-Kx^{2}\leq\frac{K^{-\delta}}{4}
, we find
x\notin(\frac{K^{-1}-\sqrt{K^{-2}-K^{-\delta+1}}}{2K},\frac{K^{-1}+\sqrt{K^{-2}-K^{-\delta+1}}}{2K})
so from (
D.2.3
),

\|g^{\star}(u)-\pi\circ g(u_{0})\|\leq\frac{K^{-\delta}}{4}

implies

\|u-g^{\star-1}\circ\pi\circ g(u_{0})\|\notin(\frac{K^{-1}-\sqrt{K^{-2}-K^{-\delta+1}}}{2K},\frac{K^{-1}+\sqrt{K^{-2}-K^{-\delta+1}}}{2K}).

From (
D.11
), we have that
\|g^{\star}(u)-\pi\circ g(u_{0})\|\leq\frac{K^{-\delta}}{4}
implies

\|u-g^{\star-1}\circ\pi\circ g(u_{0})\|\leq K^{-2}/2<\frac{K^{-1}+\sqrt{K^{-2}-K^{-\delta+1}}}{2K},

so finally
\|g^{\star}(u)-\pi\circ g(u_{0})\|\leq\frac{K^{-\delta}}{4}
implies that

\|u-g^{\star-1}\circ\pi\circ g(u_{0})\|\leq\frac{K^{-1}-\sqrt{K^{-2}-K^{-\delta+1}}}{2K}\leq\frac{K^{-\delta+2}}{2K}=\frac{K^{-\delta+1}}{2}.

Writing
C_{d}
the volume of the
d
-dimensional ball, we can conclude that

\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\{\|\pi(g^{\star}(U))-\pi(g(u_{0}))\|\leq\frac{K^{-\delta}}{4}\}}]\leq C_{d}\left(\frac{K^{-\delta+1}}{2}\right)^{d}.

Finally, from (
D.2.3
) we have

\displaystyle W_{1}(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[\frac{K^{-\delta}}{8}\mathds{1}_{\{\|\pi(g(U))-\pi(g(u_{0}))\|\leq\frac{K^{-\delta}}{8}\}}-\frac{K^{-\delta}}{4}\mathds{1}_{\{\|\pi(g^{\star}(U))-\pi(g(u_{0}))\|\leq\frac{K^{-\delta}}{4}\}}]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{K^{-\delta}}{8}C_{d-1}\left(\frac{K^{-(\delta+1)}}{2^{5}}\right)^{d-1}\frac{K^{-\frac{\delta+1}{2}}}{2^{6}}-\frac{K^{-\delta}}{4}C_{d}\left(\frac{K^{-\delta+1}}{2}\right)^{d}\right)
