\displaystyle=\frac{C_{d-1}K^{-\delta(d+1)}}{2^{5(d+1)}}(K^{\delta/2-d+1/2}-\frac{C_{d}2^{4d+2}K^{d}}{C_{d-1}}).

Then for
\delta>3
such that
K^{\delta/2-d+1/2}-\frac{C_{d}2^{4d+2}K^{d}}{C_{d-1}}\geq\frac{2^{5(d+1)}}{C_{d-1}}
we have

W_{1}(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\geq K^{-\delta(d+1)}

so from Proposition
D.4
we have

d_{\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1}}(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\geq CK^{-\delta(d+1)(2\beta+1)}.

∎

Recalling that in the assumptions of Proposition
D.5
we suppose that
d_{\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1}}(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\leq C^{-1}
, then supposing
C>0
large enough, using proposition
D.11
, we obtain

\inf\limits_{u\in[0,1]^{d}}\lambda_{\min}((\nabla g(u))^{\top}\nabla g(u))^{1/2}\geq C^{-1}.

(D.12)

D.2.4
The
(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})
-compatible map

We can now define the map
T:\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}^{r_{g_{s}}/4}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
by

T(x)=(\pi_{s|_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}})^{-1}\circ\pi_{s}(x),

which is well defined because
\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}},\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}})\leq Ks<r_{g_{s}}/4
. Let us now state that
T
is the map we are interested in:

Proposition D.12
.

The map
T:\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}^{t}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
defined by

T(x)=(\pi_{s|_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}})^{-1}\circ\pi_{s}(x)
, is
(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})_{K_{T}}
-compatible with radius
t\geq C^{-1}
and
K_{T}\leq C
.

Let us start by proving the point
i)
. We have

\displaystyle\nabla T(x)=\nabla(\pi_{s|_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}})^{-1}(\pi_{s}(x))\circ\nabla\pi_{s}(x).

(D.13)

From (
D.7
),
as
g^{\star}
is
K
-Lipschitz, we have that for
x\in\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
and
h\in\mathcal{T}_{x}(\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}})
,

\|\nabla\pi_{s|_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}}(x)h\|\geq K^{-1}(K^{-1}-\sqrt{8s}K)\|h\|.

(D.14)

Then, as
\pi_{s}\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{Cs^{-C_{2}}}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}^{r_{g_{s}}/4},\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}})
, from (
D.13
) and the Faa di Bruno formula, we deduce that
T\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{Cs^{-C_{2}}}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}^{r_{g_{s}}/4},\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}})
.

Let us now show that the density of
(T\circ g)_{\#U}
with respect to the volume measure on
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
, is of regularity
\beta
. As
\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}},\mathcal{M}_{g})\leq\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{M}_{g},\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}})+\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}},\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}})\leq 2Ks
we show the same way as we did with
g^{\star}
that
\pi_{s}\circ g
is a diffeomorphism and therefore,
T\circ g
is also a diffeomorphism. Let us write
f_{T}
the density of
(T\circ g)_{\#U}
with respect to the volume measure on
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
. We have

f_{T}(x)=\text{det}\big(\nabla(T\circ g)((T\circ g)^{-1}(x))^{\top}\nabla(T\circ g)((T\circ g)^{-1}(x))\big)^{-1/2}.

Using (
D.12
) and
doing the same derivations as with
g^{\star}
in the proof of Proposition
D.10
, we can show that there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
u\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
,

\lambda_{\min}(\nabla(\pi_{s}\circ g)(u)^{\top}\nabla(\pi_{s}\circ g)(u))^{1/2}\geq C^{-1}-\sqrt{16s}K.

Furthermore, for all
u\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
, as

\forall h\in\mathcal{T}_{\pi_{s}(g(u))}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}})
,

\|\nabla\pi_{s|_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}}(T\circ g(u))h\|\leq 2\|h\|,

we have

\lambda_{\min}\big(\nabla(T\circ g)((T\circ g)^{-1}(x))^{\top}\nabla(T\circ g)((T\circ g)^{-1}(x))\big)^{1/2}\geq\frac{1}{2}(C^{-1}-\sqrt{16s}K).

(D.15)

Then for
s>0
small enough, using the Faa di Bruno formula we have that
f_{T}\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta}_{Cs^{-C_{2}}}(\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}},\mathbb{R})
. Therefore point
i)
is verified.

Proposition
D.8
asserts that there exists a constant
C>0
such that
\pi_{s}
verifies
ii)
for a radius
C^{-1}
. Then, as
\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}},\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}})\leq Ks\leq C^{-1}/2
for
s>0
small enough, we deduce that
T
verifies
ii)
for a radius
t=C^{-1}/4
.

Let us prove point
iii)
. Let
f\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{Cs^{-1}}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}},(\mathbb{R}^{p})^{p-d})
such that
(f_{1}(x),...,f_{p-d}(x))
is an orthonormal basis of
\mathcal{T}_{x}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}})^{\top}
. For
x\in\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}
, define
R_{x}:\mathbb{R}^{p-d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{p}
a linear map such that
R_{x}(e_{i})=f_{i}(x)
for
(e_{1},...,e_{p-d})
the canonical basis of
\mathbb{R}^{p-d}
. Then for
h\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}^{t},\mathbb{R})
and
x\in\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{T^{-1}(\{x\})}h(y)d\lambda^{p-d}_{E_{x}}(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{T}_{\pi_{s}(x)}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}})^{\perp}\cap B^{d}(x,t)}h(y)d\lambda^{p-d}_{\mathcal{T}_{\pi_{s}(x)}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}})^{\perp}}(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p-d}\cap B^{d}(0,t)}h(x+R_{\pi_{s}(x)}(z))d\lambda^{p-d}(z).

Then as
\forall z\in\mathbb{R}^{p-d}
, the map
x\mapsto x+R_{\pi_{s}(x)}(z)
belongs to
\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{Cs^{-1}}(\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}},\mathbb{R}^{p})
, we have that
T
verifies
iii)
.

Let us prove
iv)
. We have

T_{g}^{-1}\circ T=(\pi_{s|_{\mathcal{M}_{g}}})^{-1}\circ\pi_{s}(x)

so as
\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}},\mathcal{M}_{g})\leq 2Ks
we show the same we showed that
T\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{Cs^{-C_{2}}}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}^{t},\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}})
, that
T_{g}^{-1}\circ T\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{Cs^{-C_{2}}}(\mathcal{M}_{g_{s}}^{t},\mathcal{M}_{g})
.

Let us now lower bound the approximate Jacobian of
T
. For
x\in\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}^{t}
, using (
D.14
) we have that

\inf\limits_{z\in\mathcal{T}_{T(x)}(\mathcal{M}_{g}^{\star})}\|\nabla\pi_{s}(x)\frac{z}{\|z\|}\|\geq\frac{1}{K}(K^{-1}-\sqrt{8s}K),

so as
\pi_{s}
is
2
-Lipschitz on
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}},
we deduce that

\inf\limits_{z\in\mathcal{T}_{T(x)}(\mathcal{M}_{g}^{\star})}\|\nabla T(x)\frac{z}{\|z\|}\|\geq\frac{1}{2K}(K^{-1}-\sqrt{8s}K).

Therefore, we deduce that the approximate Jacobian of
T
verifies

\text{ap}_{d}(\nabla T(x))\geq\left(\frac{1}{2K}(K^{-1}-\sqrt{16s}K)\right)^{d},

so
T
verifies the point
iv)
for
s>0
small enough.
∎

D.3
Proof of Lemma
5.2

The proof breaks down as follow. We first prove that we only need to treat the case
\gamma=1
. Then, we build a function
H\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{C}(A,\mathbb{R})
for
A
a neighborhood of
\mathcal{M}_{g}\cup\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
, and prove that
d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{1}}(g_{\#U},(T\circ g)_{\#U})\leq\mathbb{E}[H(g(U))]^{\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+1}}
. Finally, we extend
H
to the whole
\mathbb{R}^{p}
space.

D.3.1
Proof that we only need to treat the case
\gamma=1

Let us first show an interpolation inequality on the distance
d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\gamma}}
.

Lemma D.13
.

For
\gamma\in(1,\beta+1)
we have for all
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\gamma}}(g_{\#U},(T\circ g)_{\#U})\leq

\displaystyle C\log(\epsilon^{-1})^{2}d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{1}}(g_{\#U},(T\circ g)_{\#U})^{\frac{\beta+1-\gamma}{\beta}}

\displaystyle\times d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\beta+1}}(g_{\#U},(T\circ g)_{\#U})^{\frac{\gamma-1}{\beta}}+C\log(\epsilon^{-1})^{2}\epsilon.

The proof follows the same arguments than the proof of Corollary
B.2
except we are first going to give additional regularity to the discriminator so that its regularization belongs to Hölder spaces.
Let
\epsilon>0
and
f^{\epsilon}_{g},f^{\epsilon}_{T}
the densities of the
\epsilon
-envelopes of
g_{\#U},(T\circ g)_{\#U}
(see Definition
B.3
) respectively. For
D\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{1}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}[D(g(U))-D(T(g(U)))]

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}D(x)(f_{g}^{\epsilon}(x)-f^{\epsilon}_{T}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)+2\epsilon.

Using Proposition
B.4
we have that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}

\displaystyle D(x)(f_{g}^{\epsilon}(x)-f^{\epsilon}_{T}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)

\displaystyle\leq C\log(\epsilon^{-1})^{2}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(D)(x)(f_{g}^{\epsilon}(x)-f^{\epsilon}_{T}(x))d\lambda_{\mathcal{X}}(x)+C\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq C\log(\epsilon^{-1})^{2}\left(\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(D)(g(U))-\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(D)(T(g(U)))]+C\epsilon\right).

Let us write
F=\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(D)
and let
\xi>0
, then

\mathbb{E}[F(g(U))-F(T(g(U)))]\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}F(x)(f_{g}^{\xi}(x)-f^{\xi}_{T}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)+2\xi.

Using Proposition
B.1
for
h_{1}=F
,
h_{2}=f_{g}^{\xi}-f^{\xi}_{T}
,
s_{1}=\gamma
,
\tau=\gamma-1
,
b_{1}=-2
,
s_{2}=b_{2}=0
,
t=r=1

and
q=\frac{\beta+1-\gamma}{\beta}
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}F(x)(f_{g}^{\xi}(x)-f^{\xi}_{T}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int\tilde{\Gamma}^{\gamma-1}(F)(f_{g}^{\xi}(x)-f^{\xi}_{T}(x))\right)^{\frac{\beta+1-\gamma}{\beta}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\int\tilde{\Gamma}^{\gamma-(\beta+1)}(F)(f_{g}^{\xi}(x)-f^{\xi}_{T}(x))\right)^{\frac{\gamma-1}{\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Gamma}^{\gamma-1}(F)(g(U))-\tilde{\Gamma}^{\gamma-1}(F)(T(g(U)))]^{\frac{\beta+1-\gamma}{\beta}}

\displaystyle\times\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Gamma}^{\gamma-(\beta+1)}(F)(g(U))-\tilde{\Gamma}^{\gamma-(\beta+1)}(F)(T(g(U)))]^{\frac{\gamma-1}{\beta}}

\displaystyle+C(\xi^{\frac{\beta+1-\gamma}{\beta}}+\xi^{\frac{\gamma-1}{\beta}}).

As
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\gamma-1}(F)\in\mathcal{B}^{1,2}_{\infty,\infty}(C)\subset\mathcal{H}^{1}_{C}
and
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\gamma-(\beta+1)}(F)\in\mathcal{B}^{\beta+1,2}_{\infty,\infty}(C)\subset\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{C}
, letting
\xi
tend to 0 we get the result.
∎

Now if we prove,

\displaystyle d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{1}}(g_{\#U},(T\circ g)_{\#U})

\displaystyle\leq C\log(\epsilon^{-1})^{2}\left(d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\beta+1}}(g_{\#U},(T\circ g)_{\#U})^{\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+1}}+C\epsilon\right),

then as
\frac{\beta+1-\gamma}{\beta}\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+1}+\frac{\gamma-1}{\beta}=\frac{\beta+\gamma}{2\beta+1}
, using lemma
D.13
we will have that

\displaystyle d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\gamma}}(g_{\#U},(T\circ g)_{\#U})

\displaystyle\leq C\log(\epsilon^{-1})^{4}\left(d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\beta+1}}(g_{\#U},(T\circ g)_{\#U})^{\frac{\beta+\gamma}{2\beta+1}}+C\epsilon\right).

Therefore, we can conclude that we only need to treat the case
\gamma=1
.

D.3.2
Construction of a good potential of regularity
\beta+1

Let us write

\omega_{g}=\inf\limits_{u\in\mathbb{T}^{d}}\inf\limits_{w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\nabla g(u)\frac{w}{\|w\|}\|\wedge 1.

From Proposition
D.11
, we have that
\omega_{g}\geq C^{-1}
so
g
is a local diffeomorphism and as it is injective, it is a diffeomorphism. Let us show that
g
is a
C^{\beta+1}
diffeomorphism.

Lemma D.14
.

We have
g^{-1}\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{C}(\mathcal{M}_{g},\mathbb{T}^{d}).

As
\nabla g^{-1}(x)=(\nabla g)^{-1}(g^{-1}(x))
and
\omega_{g}\geq C
, we deduce from the Faa di Bruno formula that
g^{-1}\in\mathcal{H}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}_{C}(\mathcal{M}_{g},\mathbb{T}^{d}).
Furthermore, we have
\nabla^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}(g^{-1})(x)=F((\nabla g)^{-1},\nabla^{2}g,...,\nabla^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}g)(g^{-1}(x))
for
F
multilinear that can be computed from the Faa di Bruno formula. Therefore we have
u\mapsto F((\nabla g)^{-1},\nabla^{2}g,...,\nabla^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}g)(u)
belongs to
\mathcal{H}^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}_{C}
. Let us now show that there exists a constant
C_{0}>0
such that
g^{-1}
is
C_{0}
-Lipschitz for the Euclidean norm. Let
x,y\in\mathcal{M}_{g}
, if
\|x-y\|\geq C_{0}^{-1}
then

\|g^{-1}(x)-g^{-1}(y)\|\leq 1\leq C_{0}\|x-y\|.

If now
\|x-y\|\leq C_{0}^{-1}
and suppose that
\|g^{-1}(x)-g^{-1}(y)\|\geq(4K)^{-1}\omega_{g}
. Then taking
C_{0}^{-1}\leq\frac{1}{4}K^{-1}w_{g}^{2}
we have from Lemma
D.19
that

\displaystyle r_{g}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|g(g^{-1}(x))-g(g^{-1}(y))\|=\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|\leq\frac{1}{8}K^{-1}w_{g}^{2}

which is impossible as
r_{g}\geq 1/K
by hypothesis. Therefore we have
\|g^{-1}(x)-g^{-1}(y)\|<(4K)^{-1}\omega_{g}
, so

\displaystyle\|x-y\|

\displaystyle=\|g(g^{-1}(x))-g(g^{-1}(y))\|\geq\omega_{g}\|g^{-1}(x)-g^{-1}(y)\|-K\|g^{-1}(x)-g^{-1}(y)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\omega_{g}\|g^{-1}(x)-g^{-1}(y)\|,

so we conclude that
g^{-1}
is
C_{0}
-Lipschitz.
Finally we have

\displaystyle|\nabla^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}

\displaystyle g^{-1}(x)-\nabla^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}g^{-1}(y)|

\displaystyle=|F((\nabla g)^{-1},\nabla^{2}g,...,\nabla^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}g)(g^{-1}(x))-F((\nabla g)^{-1},\nabla^{2}g,...,\nabla^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}g)(g^{-1}(y))|

\displaystyle\leq C\|g^{-1}(x)-g^{-1}(y)\|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}

\displaystyle\leq C\|x-y\|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}.

∎Applying Proposition
D.5
, we have that there exists a map T being
(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})_{K_{T}}
-compatible with radius
t\geq C^{-1}
and
K_{T}\leq C
. For the lightness of the derivations, let us write

X(u)=g(u)-T(g(u)).

Then
for
D\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{1}
we have

\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}\Big[D(g(U))-D(T(g(U)))\Big]\leq C\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}\left[\|X(U)\|\right].

(D.16)

Let
\xi>0
, and define
L:\mathbb{T}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{p}
by

\displaystyle L(u)=\frac{X(u)}{\|X(u)\|}\big(1-(1-\|X(u)\|/\xi)\mathds{1}_{\{\|X(u)\|<\xi\}}\big).

(D.17)

Let us write
K_{X}=\|X\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}}\vee 1\leq C
and
\delta_{u}=\|X(u)\|
for
u\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
.
We have that

L\in\mathcal{H}^{0}_{1}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})
, let us show that
L
has some additional Hölder regularity.

Proposition D.15
.

For
L:\mathbb{T}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{p}
defined in (
D.17
), we have for all
\alpha\in[0,1]
and
u,v\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
,

\|L(u)-L(v)\|\leq 8K_{X}\min(\delta_{u},\delta_{v})^{-\alpha}\|u-v\|^{\alpha}.

Suppose first that
\|u-v\|\geq\min(\delta_{u},\delta_{v})/(4K_{X})
. Then

\displaystyle\|L(u)-L(v)\|

\displaystyle\leq 2=2\|u-v\|^{-\alpha}\|u-v\|^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq 8K_{X}\min(\delta_{u},\delta_{v})^{-\alpha}\|u-v\|^{\alpha}.

Now if
\|u-v\|\leq\min(\delta_{u},\delta_{v})/(4K_{X})
, then for
u_{s}=u+s(v-u)
with
s\in[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\|X(u_{s})\|

\displaystyle\geq\|X(u)\|-sK_{X}\|u-v\|

\displaystyle\geq\delta_{u}-\frac{1}{4}\min(\delta_{u},\delta_{v})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\min(\delta_{u},\delta_{v}).

Furthermore, the differential of
L
is defined almost everywhere and values

\displaystyle\nabla L(u)=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\|X(u)\|\geq\xi\}}}{\|X(u)\|}\Big(\nabla X(u)-\frac{X(u)}{\|X(u)\|}\frac{(X(u))^{\top}}{\|X(u)\|}\nabla X(u)\Big)+\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\|X(u)\|<\xi\}}}{\xi}\nabla X(u),

so
\|\nabla L(u)\|\leq K_{X}\|X(u)\|^{-1}=K_{X}\delta_{u}^{-1}
.
Then

\displaystyle\|L(u)-L(v)\|

\displaystyle=\|\int_{0}^{1}\nabla L(u+s(v-u))(v-u)ds\|

\displaystyle\leq\|v-u\|\int_{0}^{1}\|\nabla L(u+s(v-u))\|ds\leq 2K_{X}\min(\delta_{u},\delta_{v})^{-1}\|v-u\|

\displaystyle\leq 2K_{X}\min(\delta_{u},\delta_{v})^{-\alpha}\|u-v\|^{\alpha}.

∎

Note that we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}\left[\|X(U)\|\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}\left[\left\langle\frac{X(U)}{\|X(U)\|},X(U))\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}\left[\left\langle L(U),X(U))\right\rangle\right]+\xi,

so we are now going to focus on finding a function
H\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{C}(\mathbb{R}^{p},\mathbb{R})
such that

\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}\left[\left\langle L(U),X(U)\right\rangle\right]\leq C\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}\left[H(g(U))-H(T\circ g(U))\right]^{\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+1}},

up to logarithmic terms. Let us define

A=\{x\in\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}^{t}|\ \|x-T(x)\|\leq\|T_{g}^{-1}\circ T(x)-T(x)\|\}

(D.18)

and

H:A\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

H(x)=\Big\langle\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L)\circ g^{-1}\circ T_{g}^{-1}\circ T(x)\ ,\ x-T(x)\Big\rangle,

(D.19)

for
T_{g}^{-1}:\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{g}
the inverse application from Definition
D.3
. We have the following result on
H
.

Proposition D.16
.

The application
H
belongs to
\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{C}(A,\mathbb{R})
and for all
\epsilon\in(0,1)
we have

\mathbb{E}_{U}\left[\left\langle L(U),X(U)\right\rangle\right]\leq C\log(\epsilon^{-1})^{2}\mathbb{E}_{U}\left[H(g(U))-H(T\circ g(U))\right]^{\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+1}}+C\epsilon.

Let

\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(L_{i})(u)=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\log(\lfloor\epsilon^{-2}\rfloor)}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}(1+j)^{-2}\alpha_{L_{i}}(j,l,z)S(j,l,w)_{i}\psi^{per}_{jlz}(u)

with
S(j,l,w)_{i}=\frac{\alpha_{L_{i}}(j,l,z)\alpha_{X_{i}}(j,l,z)}{|\alpha_{L_{i}}(j,l,z)\alpha_{X_{i}}(j,l,z)|}
.
Then using Proposition
B.4
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}[\langle L(U),X(U)\rangle]\leq C\log(\epsilon^{-1})^{2}\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}[\langle\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(L)(U),X(U)\rangle]+C\epsilon.

Applying Proposition
B.1
for
h_{1}=\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(L)
,
h_{2}=X
,
s_{1}=0
,
b_{1}=-2
,
s_{2}=\beta+1
,
b_{2}=0
,
\tau=-\beta
,
t=1
,
r=0
and
q=\frac{2\beta+1}{\beta+1}
we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{U}[\langle\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(L)(U),X(U)\rangle]

\displaystyle\leq\Big\langle\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta}(\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(L)),X\Big\rangle_{L^{2}}^{\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+1}}\Big\langle\tilde{\Gamma}^{0}(\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(L)),\Gamma^{\beta+1}(X)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}}^{\frac{\beta}{2\beta+1}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\Big\langle\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L),X\Big\rangle_{L^{2}}+C\epsilon^{2\beta+1}\right)^{\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+1}}\Big\langle\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(L),\Gamma^{\beta+1}(X)\Big\rangle_{L^{2}}^{\frac{\beta}{2\beta+1}},

where we used that

\Big\langle\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta}(\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(L)),X\Big\rangle_{L^{2}}=\Big\langle\tilde{\Gamma}_{\epsilon}^{-\beta,-2}(L),X\Big\rangle_{L^{2}}\leq\Big\langle\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L),X\Big\rangle_{L^{2}}+C\epsilon^{2\beta+1}.

We have that
\Gamma^{\beta+1}(X)\in\mathcal{B}^{0}_{\infty,\infty}(C)
and
\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(L)\in\mathcal{B}^{0,2}_{\infty,\infty}(C)
so

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\alpha_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(L_{i})}(j,l,z)\alpha_{\Gamma^{\beta+1}(X_{i})}(j,l,z)

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}(1+j)^{-2}C2^{-jd}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{-2}\leq C.

Let us show that
\nabla^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}_{\epsilon}(L)
exists and has some Hölder regularity. Let
u\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
be such that
\delta_{u}>\xi
and define
L_{u}:B^{d}(u,\frac{\delta_{u}}{4K_{X}})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{p}
being equal to
L
. We then extend
L_{u}
to the whole cube by

\overline{L}_{u}(v)=L_{u}(\pi_{B_{u}}(v))(0\vee(1-3\|v-\pi_{B_{u}}(v)\|)),

(D.20)

for
\pi_{B_{u}}
the projection on
B^{d}(u,\frac{\delta_{u}}{4K_{X}})
(recall that it is the periodised Euclidean norm we are using). For all
v\in B^{d}(u,\frac{\delta_{u}}{4K_{X}})
, we have
\delta_{v}\geq\delta_{u}/2
so from Proposition
D.15
with
\alpha=1-(\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor)
we get

L_{u}\in\mathcal{H}^{0}_{1}(B^{d}(u,\frac{\delta_{u}}{4K_{X}}),\mathbb{R}^{p})\cap\mathcal{H}^{1-(\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor)}_{16K_{X}\delta_{u}^{-(1-(\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor))}}(B^{d}(u,\frac{\delta_{u}}{4K_{X}}),\mathbb{R}^{p}).

Therefore, we also have that

\overline{L}_{u}\in\mathcal{H}^{0}_{1}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})\cap\mathcal{H}^{1-(\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor)}_{16K_{X}\delta_{u}^{-(1-(\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor))}}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p}).

Let us look at the size of the support of the periodised wavelet. Recall from (
A.9
) that if
2^{j}>N
, for
s\in[0,1]
and
r\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}
, the one-dimensional periodised scaling and wavelet functions equal

\phi^{per}_{j}(s-2^{-j}z)=\phi(2^{j}s-z)+\phi(2^{j}(s+1)-z)

and

\psi^{per}_{j}(s-2^{-j}z)=\psi(2^{j}(s-1)-z)+\psi(2^{j}s-z)+\psi(2^{j}(s+1)-z).

For
l_{i}\in\{0,1\}
, as
supp(\psi_{l_{i}}(2^{j}\cdot-r))\subset[(r-N)2^{-j},(r+N)2^{-j}]
we have:

if
z_{i}\geq 2^{j}-N
,

\displaystyle[0,1]\cap supp(\psi_{l_{i}}(2^{j}\cdot-z_{i}))

\displaystyle\subset[(z_{i}-N)2^{-j},1]

\displaystyle[0,1]\cap supp(\psi_{l_{i}}(2^{j}(\cdot+1)-z_{i}))

\displaystyle\subset[0,(z_{i}+N)2^{-j}-1]

and if
z_{i}\leq N
,

\displaystyle[0,1]\cap supp(\psi_{l_{i}}(2^{j}(\cdot-1)-z_{i}))

\displaystyle\subset[1+(z_{i}-N)2^{-j},1]

\displaystyle[0,1]\cap supp(\psi_{l_{i}}(2^{j}\cdot-z_{i}))

\displaystyle\subset[0,(z_{i}+N)2^{-j}].

Then
supp(\psi_{l_{i}}^{per}(2^{j}\cdot-z_{i}))\subset[(z_{i}-N)2^{-j},(z_{i}+N)2^{-j}]/\mathbb{Z}
so finally we can conclude that

supp(\psi_{j,l,z}^{per})\subset B^{d}(z2^{-j},Nd2^{-j}),

recalling that this is the ball for the periodised Euclidean distance.

Let us write
(\alpha_{(\overline{L}_{u})_{i}}(j,l,z))_{(j,l,z)}
the wavelet coefficients of
(\overline{L}_{u})_{i}
for
i=1,...,p
. As
\text{support}(\psi^{per}_{jlz})\subset B^{d}(2^{-j}z,dN2^{-j})
, we have that for any
(j,l,z)
such that

j>\lfloor\log_{2}(dN4K_{X}\delta_{u}^{-1})\rfloor+1
and
\text{support}(\psi^{per}_{jlz})\cap B^{d}(u,\frac{\delta_{u}}{8K_{X}})\neq\varnothing
then

\alpha_{(\overline{L}_{u})_{i}}(j,l,z)=\alpha_{L_{i}}(j,l,z).

Let us note

\xi_{u}=\lfloor\log_{2}(dN4K_{X}\delta_{u}^{-1})\rfloor+1

and define the map
\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(\overline{L}_{u})_{i}:\mathbb{T}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(\overline{L}_{u})_{i}(v)=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}2^{-j\beta}(1+j)^{-2}S(j,l,z)_{i}\alpha_{(\overline{L}_{u})_{i}}(j,l,z)\psi^{per}_{j,l,z}(v),

the
\beta,2
wavelet regularization of
\overline{L}_{u_{i}}
. Then

\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(\overline{L}_{u})\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta}_{C}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})\cap\mathcal{H}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}_{CK_{X}\delta_{u}^{-(1-(\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor))}}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p}).

Let
\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\in\{1,...,d\}
, we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}

\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L)_{i}(u)|=|\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\alpha_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L_{i})}(j,l,z)\psi_{j,l,z}^{per}(u)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\xi_{u}}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}(\alpha_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L_{i})}(j,l,z)-\alpha_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(\overline{L}_{u})_{i}}(j,l,z))\psi_{j,l,z}^{per}(u)|

\displaystyle+|\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\xi_{u}}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\alpha_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(\overline{L}_{u})_{i}}(j,l,z)\psi_{j,l,z}^{per}(u)

\displaystyle+\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\sum\limits_{j=\xi_{u}+1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\alpha_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L_{i})}(j,l,z)\psi_{j,l,z}^{per}(u)|.

(D.21)

For
k\in\{1,...,d\}
, let us write
\theta_{k}=\sum\limits_{r=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\mathds{1}_{\{i_{r}=k\}}
. For the first term of (
D.3.2
) we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\xi_{u}}

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}(\alpha_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L_{i})}(j,l,z)-\alpha_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(\overline{L}_{u})_{i}}(j,l,z))\psi_{j,l,z}^{per}(u)|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\xi_{u}}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}(\alpha_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L_{i})}(j,l,z)-\alpha_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(\overline{L}_{u})_{i}}(j,l,z))

\displaystyle\times 2^{j(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1+d/2)}\prod\limits_{k=1}^{d}\sum\limits_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\partial^{\theta_{k}}\psi_{l_{k}}(2^{j}(u_{k}-m)-z_{k})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\xi_{u}}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}2^{j(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1-\beta)}|\prod\limits_{k=1}^{d}\sum\limits_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\partial^{\theta_{k}}\psi_{l_{k}}(2^{j}(u_{k}-m)-z_{k})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\xi_{u}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}2^{j(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1-\beta)}\mathds{1}_{\{u\in supp(\psi_{jlz}^{per})\}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\xi_{u}}2^{j(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1-\beta)}\leq C2^{\xi_{u}(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1-\beta)}\leq CK_{X}\delta_{u}^{-(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1-\beta)}.

For the second term we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\xi_{u}}

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\alpha_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(\overline{L}_{u})_{i}}(j,l,z)\psi_{j,l,z}^{per}(u)

\displaystyle+\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\sum\limits_{j=\xi_{u}+1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\alpha_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L_{i})}(j,l,z)\psi_{j,l,z}^{per}(u)|

\displaystyle=|\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\alpha_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(\overline{L}_{u})_{i}}(j,l,z)\psi_{j,l,z}^{per}(u)|

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(\overline{L}_{u})_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq CK_{X}\delta_{u}^{-(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1-\beta)}.

The same way, let us show that for
u,v\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
we have

|\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L)_{i}(u)-\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L)_{i}(v)|\leq CK_{X}(\delta_{u}^{-1}\vee\delta_{v}^{-1})\|u-v\|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}.

(D.22)

Suppose that
\|u-v\|\leq\frac{\delta_{u}\vee\delta_{v}}{8K_{X}}
, then define
L_{u,v}
coinciding with
L
on
B^{d}(\argmax\limits_{w\in\{u,v\}}\delta_{w},\frac{\delta_{u}\vee\delta_{v}}{8K_{X}})
. Otherwise, if
\|u-v\|>\frac{\delta_{u}\vee\delta_{v}}{8K_{X}}
define
L_{u,v}
coinciding with
L
on
B^{d}(u,\frac{\delta_{u}}{16K_{X}})\cup B^{d}(v,\frac{\delta_{v}}{16K_{X}})
. In both cases we have
L_{u,v}\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{16K_{X}\delta_{u}^{-1}\vee\delta_{v}^{-1}}
. In the first case extend
L_{u,v}
by projection on the ball as we did with
L_{u}
in (
D.20
), in the second case we extend it with the sum of the projections:

\displaystyle\overline{L}_{u,v}(w)=

\displaystyle L_{u,v}(\pi_{B_{u}}(w))(0\vee(1-24K_{X}\delta_{u}^{-1}\|w-\pi_{B_{u}}(w)\|))

\displaystyle+L_{u,v}(\pi_{B_{v}}(w))(0\vee(1-24K_{X}\delta_{v}^{-1}\|w-\pi_{B_{v}}(w)\|)).

In both cases we also have
\overline{L}_{u,v}\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{C\delta_{u}^{-1}\vee\delta_{v}^{-1}}
. Then by splitting the wavelet coefficients of
\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L)_{i}(u)-\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L)_{i}(v)
as in (
D.3.2
), we obtain (
D.22
).

Let
A
and

H:A\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
defined in (
D.18
) and (
D.19
). We have
g^{-1}\circ T_{g}^{-1}\circ T\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{C}(A,\mathbb{T}^{d})
,
Id-T\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{K_{T}}(\mathbb{R}^{p},\mathbb{R}^{p})
and
\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L)\in\mathcal{H}_{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})
, so we get from the Faa di Bruno formula that
H\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta}_{C}(A,\mathbb{R})
. Let
\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\in\{1,...,p\}
and write
P(\{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\})
the set of subsets of
\{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\}
. We have

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}H(x)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{S\in P(\{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\})}\Big\langle\partial^{|S|}_{i\in S}(\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L)\circ g^{-1}\circ T_{g}^{-1}\circ T)(x)\ ,\ \partial^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1-|S|}_{i\notin S}(\text{Id}-T)(x)\Big\rangle.

For all
S\in P(\{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\})
with
|S|<\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1
, we have that

x\mapsto\Big\langle\partial^{|S|}_{i\in S}(\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L)\circ g^{-1}\circ T_{g}^{-1}\circ T)(x)\ ,\ \partial^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1-|S|}_{i\notin S}(\text{Id}-T)(x)\Big\rangle\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}_{C}(A,\mathbb{R}).

From the previous calculations, we know that

\displaystyle|\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}

\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L)_{i}(g^{-1}\circ T_{g}^{-1}\circ T(x))|

\displaystyle\leq CK_{X}\delta_{g^{-1}\circ T_{g}^{-1}\circ T(x)}^{-(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1-\beta)}

\displaystyle=CK_{X}\|T_{g}^{-1}\circ T(x)-T(T_{g}^{-1}\circ T(x))\|^{-(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1-\beta)}

\displaystyle=CK_{X}\|T_{g}^{-1}\circ T(x)-T(x)\|^{-(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1-\beta)}

\displaystyle\leq CK_{X}\|x-T(x)\|^{-(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1-\beta)}

so

|\Big\langle\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}(\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L)\circ g^{-1}\circ T_{g}^{-1}\circ T)(x)\ ,(\text{Id}-T)(x)\Big\rangle|\leq CK_{X}^{C_{3}}\|x-T(x)\|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}.

Let
x,y\in A
with
\|x-T(x)\|\leq\|y-T(y)\|
, from (
D.22
) we get

\displaystyle|

\displaystyle\Big\langle\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}(\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L)\circ g^{-1}\circ T_{g}^{-1}\circ T)(x)\ ,(\text{Id}-T)(x)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle-\Big\langle\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}(\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L)\circ g^{-1}\circ T_{g}^{-1}\circ T)(y)\ ,(\text{Id}-T)(y)\Big\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\Big\langle\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}(\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L)\circ g^{-1}\circ T_{g}^{-1}\circ T)(x)

\displaystyle-\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}(\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L)\circ g^{-1}\circ T_{g}^{-1}\circ T)(y)\ ,(\text{Id}-T)(x)\Big\rangle|

\displaystyle+|\Big\langle\partial_{\mathbb{i}_{1},...,\mathbb{i}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}(\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L)\circ g^{-1}\circ T_{g}^{-1}\circ T)(y)\ ,(\text{Id}-T)(x)-(\text{Id}-T)(y)\Big\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CK_{X}^{C_{3}}\min(\|x-T(x)\|,\|y-T(y)\|)^{-1}\|x-y\|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\|x-T(x)\|

\displaystyle+\|T_{g}^{-1}\circ T(y)-T(y)\|^{-(1-(\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor))}\|(\text{Id}-T)(x)-(\text{Id}-T)(y)\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CK_{X}^{C_{3}}\|x-y\|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}+\|y-T(y)\|^{-(1-(\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor))}\|(\text{Id}-T)(x)-(\text{Id}-T)(y)\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|x-y\|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}

\displaystyle+\|y-T(y)\|^{-(1-(\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor))}2\|y-T(y)\|^{1-(\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor)}\|(\text{Id}-T)(x)-(\text{Id}-T)(y)\|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|x-y\|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}.

Therefore we have finally that
H\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{C}(A,\mathbb{R})
.
∎

D.3.3
Extension of the potential to the whole
\mathbb{R}^{p}
space

Let us now prove that we can extend
H
to the whole
\mathbb{R}^{p}
using Whitney’s extension Theorem (Theorem
D.20
).

Proposition D.17
.

The map
H\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{C}(A,\mathbb{R})
can be extended into a map in
\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{C}(\mathbb{R}^{p},\mathbb{R})
.

Let
x_{1},...,x_{k}\in A
and define

P_{i}(x)=\sum\limits_{l=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\nabla^{l}H(x_{i})\frac{(x-x_{i})^{l}}{l!},

with the notations
\nabla^{l}H(x_{i})
the
l
-differential of
H
and

\nabla^{l}H(x_{i})(x-x_{i})^{l}=\nabla^{l}H(x_{i})\big(x-x_{i},x-x_{i},...,x-x_{i}).

Then the
P_{i}
check conditions a) and b) for
M=\|H\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}(A,\mathbb{R})}
. Let show that they also verify condition c). First, for
\alpha=\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1
we have

\|\nabla^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}P_{i}(x_{i})-\nabla^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}P_{j}(x_{i})\|=\|\nabla^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}H(x_{i})-\nabla^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}H(x_{j})\|\leq C\|x_{i}-x_{j}\|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}.

Let
\alpha\in\{0,...,\lfloor\beta\rfloor\}
and
\gamma:[0,1]\rightarrow A
continuous and differentiable almost everywhere such that
\gamma(0)=x_{j}
and
\gamma(1)=x_{i}
.
Then, from Lemma
D.21
we get

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{\alpha}

\displaystyle P_{i}(x_{i})-\nabla^{\alpha}P_{j}(x_{i})\|=\|\nabla^{\alpha}H(x_{i})-\sum\limits_{l=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1-\alpha}\nabla^{l+\alpha}H(x_{j})\frac{(x_{i}-x_{j})^{l}}{l!}\|\leq C\|\dot{\gamma}\|^{\beta+1-\alpha}_{L^{1}}.

Let us now define a path from
x_{i}
to
x_{j}
. Suppose first that
\|x_{i}-x_{j}\|\leq t/4
(for
t>0
the radius of definition of
T:\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}^{t}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
), and define
P_{A}:\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}^{t}\rightarrow A
by

P_{A}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}x&\text{if }x\in A\\
\frac{x-T(x)}{\|x-T(x)\|}\|T_{g}^{-1}\circ T(x)-T(x)\|+T(x)&\text{if }x\notin A\end{array}\right.

For
x\notin A
we have

\displaystyle\nabla P_{A}(x)=

\displaystyle\frac{\|T_{g}^{-1}\circ T(x)-T(x)\|}{\|x-T(x)\|}\left(\nabla(\text{Id}-T)(x)-\frac{x-T(x)}{\|x-T(x)\|}\frac{(x-T(x))^{\top}}{\|x-T(x)\|}\nabla(\text{Id}-T)(x)\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{x-T(x)}{\|x-T(x)\|}\frac{(x-T(x))^{\top}}{\|x-T(x)\|}\nabla(T_{g}^{-1}\circ T-T)(x)+\nabla T(x),

so
\|\nabla P_{A}(x)\|\leq 4K_{T}^{2}
. Let
\gamma:[0,1]\rightarrow A
defined by

\gamma(s)=P_{A}(x_{i}+s(x_{j}-x_{i})),

which is well defined as for
s\in[0,1]
,

d(x_{i}+s(x_{j}-x_{i}),\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}})\leq\|x_{i}+s(x_{j}-x_{i})-x_{i}\|+d(x_{i},\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}})\leq t/4+t/2.

We have
\|\dot{\gamma}(s)\|\leq 4K_{T}^{2}\|x_{i}-x_{j}\|
so we deduce that

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\nabla^{\alpha}P_{i}(x_{i})-\nabla^{\alpha}P_{j}(x_{i})\|\leq C\|x_{i}-x_{j}\|^{\beta+1-\alpha}.

Suppose now that
\|x_{i}-x_{j}\|\geq t/4
and define

\gamma(s)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}x_{i}+3s(\pi_{g^{\star}}(x_{i})-x_{i})&\text{if }s\in[0,1/3]\\
g^{\star}\Big(g^{\star-1}(\pi_{g^{\star}}(x_{i}))+3(s-1/3)(g^{\star-1}(\pi_{g^{\star}}(x_{j}))-g^{\star-1}(\pi_{g^{\star}}(x_{i}))\Big)&\text{if }s\in(1/3,2/3)\\
\pi_{g^{\star}}(x_{j})+3(s-2/3)(x_{j}-\pi_{g^{\star}}(x_{j}))&\text{if }s\in[2/3,1],\end{array}\right.

the path from
x_{i}
to
x_{j}
that starts by projecting
x_{i}
onto
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
, then circulates along
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
until it reaches the projection of
x_{j}
, and finally go to
x_{j}
in a straight line.

For
s\in[0,1/3]\cup[2/3,1]
we have

\|\dot{\gamma}(s)\|\leq 3\max(\|\pi_{g^{\star}}(x_{i})-x_{i}\|,\|\pi_{g^{\star}}(x_{j})-x_{j}\|)\leq 3t\leq 12\|x_{i}-x_{j}\|

and for
s\in(1/3,2/3)

\|\dot{\gamma}(s)\|\leq 3K^{2}\|\pi_{g^{\star}}(x_{i})-\pi_{g^{\star}}(x_{j})\|\leq 3K^{3}\leq 12K^{3}t^{-1}\|x_{i}-x_{j}\|.

We then get as in the case where
\|x_{i}-x_{j}\|\leq t/4
, that

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{\alpha}P_{i}(x_{i})-\nabla^{\alpha}P_{j}(x_{i})\|\leq

\displaystyle C\|x_{i}-x_{j}\|^{\beta+1-\alpha}.

Therefore using the sharp form of Whitney’s extension Theorem
D.20
,
H
can be extended to a map in
\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{C}(\mathbb{R}^{p},\mathbb{R})
.
∎

We conclude the proof of Lemma
5.2
by noticing that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{U}[\langle\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\beta,-2}(L)(U),g(U)-T(g(U))\rangle]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{U}[H(g(U))-H(T(g(U)))]

\displaystyle\leq C\sup\limits_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1},\ f\circ T=0}\mathbb{E}_{U}[f(g(U))-f(T(g(U)))].

D.4
Proof of Lemma
5.3

By the point
i)
of definition of the
(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})
compatibility (Defintion
D.3
), we have that
T\circ g_{\#U}
admits a density
f_{T}\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta}_{K_{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}},\mathbb{R})
with respect to the volume measure on
\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}
.
Define the
t
-envelope of
f_{T}
and
f_{g^{\star}}
as in Definition
B.3
but in the case where the densities have some regularity:

f_{T}^{t}(x)=\frac{f_{T}(T(x))\Theta(4\|x-T(x)\|^{2}/t^{2})|\text{ap}_{d}(\nabla T(x))|}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p-d}}\Theta(4\|y\|^{2}/t^{2})d\lambda^{p-d}(y)}

and

f_{g^{\star}}^{t}(x)=\frac{f_{g^{\star}}(T(x))\Theta(4\|x-T(x)\|^{2}/t^{2})|\text{ap}_{d}(\nabla T(x))|}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p-d}}\Theta(4\|y\|^{2}/t^{2})d\lambda^{p-d}(y)},

for
\text{ap}_{d}
the approximate jacobian from Definition
D.1
and
\Theta\in\mathcal{H}_{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R}_{+})
such that
\Theta(x)=\Theta(-x)
,
\Theta(0)=1
and
\Theta_{|(1,\infty)}=0
.

As
\nabla T
belongs to
\mathcal{H}_{K_{T}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{p},L(\mathbb{R}^{p},\mathbb{R}^{p}))
and
\text{ap}_{d}(\nabla T(x))
is bounded below by
C^{-1}
(
T
verifies point (iv) of Definition
D.3
), then from the Faa di Bruno formula, we deduce that
\text{ap}_{d}(\nabla T)
belongs to
\mathcal{H}_{C}^{\beta}(\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}^{t},\mathbb{R})
. Let
D\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{C}(\mathbb{R}^{p},\mathbb{R})
and define
\overline{D}:\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}^{t}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

\overline{D}(x)=D(T(x))\kappa(2\|x-T(x)\|^{2}/t^{2}),

for
\kappa\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{C}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R})
such that
\kappa_{|(-\infty,1/2)}=1
and
\kappa_{|(1,\infty)}=0
. Then, extending
\overline{D}
by 0 to the whole space
\mathbb{R}^{p}
, we have that
\overline{D}\in\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{C}(\mathbb{R}^{p},\mathbb{R})
. From Proposition
D.2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{U}

\displaystyle[D(T(g(U)))-D(g^{\star}(U))]=\int_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}D(x)(f_{T}(x)-f_{g^{\star}}(x))d\lambda_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}\frac{D(x)(f_{T}(x)-f_{g^{\star}}(x))}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p-d}}\Theta(4\|y\|^{2}/t^{2})d\lambda^{p-d}(y)}\int_{T^{-1}(\{x\})}\Theta(4\|z-T(z)\|^{2}/t^{2})d\lambda^{p-d}(z)d\lambda_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}\int_{T^{-1}(\{x\})}

\displaystyle\frac{D(T(z))(f_{T}(T(z))-f_{g^{\star}}(T(z)))\Theta(4\|z-T(z)\|^{2}/t^{2})}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p-d}}\Theta(4\|y\|^{2}/t^{2})d\lambda^{p-d}(y)}d\lambda^{p-d}(z)d\lambda_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}^{t}}D(T(x))(f_{T}^{t}(T(x))-f_{g^{\star}}^{t}(T(x)))d\lambda^{p}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\overline{D}(x)(f_{T}^{t}(x)-f_{g^{\star}}^{t}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x).

Let

\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(\overline{D})(x)=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\log(\lfloor\epsilon^{-2}\rfloor)}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}(1+j)^{-2}S(j,l,w)\alpha_{\overline{D}}(j,l,w)\psi_{jlw}(x)

for
S(j,l,w)=\frac{\alpha_{\overline{D}}(j,l,w)(\alpha_{f_{T}^{t}}(j,l,w)-\alpha_{f_{g^{\star}}^{t}}(j,l,w))}{|\alpha_{\overline{D}}(j,l,w)(\alpha_{f_{T}^{t}}(j,l,w)-\alpha_{f_{g^{\star}}^{t}}(j,l,w))|}
. Then using Proposition
B.4
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}

\displaystyle\overline{D}(x)(f_{T}^{t}(x)-f_{g^{\star}}^{t}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)

\displaystyle\leq C\log(\epsilon^{-1})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(\overline{D})(f_{T}^{t}(x)-f_{g^{\star}}^{t}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)+C\epsilon.

Applying Proposition
B.1
for
h_{1}=\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(\overline{D})
,
h_{2}=f_{T}^{t}-f_{g^{\star}}^{t}
,
s_{1}=\gamma
,
b_{1}=2
,
s_{2}=\beta
,
b_{2}=0
,
\tau=-(\beta+1-\gamma)
,
t=1
,
r=\frac{\gamma}{\beta+\gamma}
and
q=\frac{2\beta+1}{\beta+\gamma}
we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(\overline{D})(x)(f_{T}^{t}(x)-f_{g^{\star}}^{t}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)\leq

\displaystyle\Big\langle\tilde{\Gamma}^{\gamma-(\beta+1),-2}_{\epsilon}(\overline{D}),f_{T}^{t}-f_{g^{\star}}^{t}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}^{\frac{\beta+\gamma}{2\beta+1}}

\displaystyle\Big\langle\tilde{\Gamma}^{\gamma,-2}_{\epsilon}(\overline{D}),\Gamma^{\beta}(f_{T}^{t}-f_{g^{\star}}^{t})\Big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}^{\frac{\beta+1-\gamma}{2\beta+1}}.

Like in the proof of lemma
5.2
, we have

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}

\displaystyle 2^{j(\beta+\gamma)}|\alpha_{\tilde{\Gamma}^{0,-2}_{\epsilon}(\overline{D})}(j,l,w)||\alpha_{f_{T}}(j,l,w)-\alpha_{f_{g^{\star}}}(j,l,w)|

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}(1+j)^{-2}C2^{-jp}\mathds{1}_{\{supp(\psi_{jlw})\cap B^{p}(0,K)\neq\varnothing\}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}(1+j)^{-2}\leq C.

Define
F_{D}:\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

F_{D}(x)=\int_{T^{-1}(\{x\})}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\gamma-(\beta+1),-2}_{\epsilon}(\overline{D})(z)\ \frac{\Theta(4\|z-T(z)\|^{2}/t^{2})}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p-d}}\Theta(4\|y\|^{2}/t^{2})d\lambda^{p-d}(y)}d\lambda_{T^{-1}(\{x\})}(z).

Then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\gamma-(\beta+1),-2}_{\epsilon}(\overline{D})(x)(f_{T}^{t}(x)-f_{g^{\star}}^{t}(x))d\lambda^{p}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}\int_{T^{-1}(\{x\})}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\gamma-(\beta+1),-2}_{\epsilon}(\overline{D})(z)(f_{T}^{t}(z)-f_{g^{\star}}^{t}(z))|\text{ap}_{d}(\nabla T(z))|^{-1}d\lambda^{p-d}(z)d\lambda_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}\int_{T^{-1}(\{x\})}\tilde{\Gamma}^{\gamma-(\beta+1),-2}_{\epsilon}(\overline{D})(z)

\displaystyle\frac{(f_{T}(T(z))-f_{g^{\star}}(T(z)))\Theta(4\|z-T(z)\|^{2}/t^{2})}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p-d}}\Theta(4\|y\|^{2}/t^{2})d\lambda^{p-d}(y)}d\lambda^{p-d}(z)d\lambda_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}F_{D}(x)(f_{T}(x)-f_{g^{\star}}(x))d\lambda_{\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}}}(x).

As
T
verifies point iii) of Definition
D.3
, we have
F_{D}\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{C}(\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}},\mathbb{R})
so we can conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}

\displaystyle[D(T(g(U)))-D(g^{\star}(U))]

\displaystyle\leq C\sup\limits_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{g^{\star}},\mathbb{R})}\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}[f(T(g(U)))-f(g^{\star}(U))]^{\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+1}}.

∎

D.5
Additional technical lemmas

D.5.1
Results on the reach of submanifolds

Let us give the characterization of the reach for submanifolds.

Lemma D.18
(
Federer 1959
, Theorem 4.18).

The reach
r\in[0,\infty)
of a submanifold
\mathcal{M}
verifies

r=\inf\limits_{q\neq p\in\mathcal{M}}\frac{\|q-p\|^{2}}{2d(q-p,\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{M}))}.

Using this result, let us show an upper bound on the reach.

Lemma D.19
.

Let
g\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{K}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R})
injective that verifies the
K_{2}
-regularity density condition and such that
\mathcal{M}_{g}=g([0,1]^{d})
is a submanifold. Then if there exist

\delta\in(0,(2K_{2}K)^{-1})
and
u^{\star},v^{\star}\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
such that

\|u^{\star}-v^{\star}\|\geq\delta\quad\text{ and }\quad\|g(u^{\star})-g(v^{\star})\|<K_{2}^{-1}\delta-K\delta^{2},

then

r_{g}\leq\|g(u^{\star})-g(v^{\star})\|/2.

For
u,v\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
we have

\displaystyle\|g(u)-g(v)\|

\displaystyle=\|\nabla g(v)(u-v)-O(\|u-v\|^{2})\|

\displaystyle\geq K_{2}^{-1}\|u-v\|-K\|u-v\|^{2}

so
\|u-v\|=\delta
implies that
\|g(u)-g(v)\|\geq K_{2}^{-1}\delta-K\delta^{2}
. Let

(x,y)\in\argmin\limits_{\|u-v\|\geq\delta}\|g(u)-g(v)\|,

then as
\|g(x)-g(y)\|\leq\|g(u^{\star})-g(v^{\star})\|<K_{2}^{-1}\delta-K\delta^{2}
, we have
\|x-y\|>\delta
. Defining
F(u,v)=\|g(u)-g(v)\|^{2}
, we have that
\nabla F(x,y)=0
so we deduce that
g(x)-g(y)\in\mathcal{T}_{g(y)}(\mathcal{M}_{g})^{\perp}.
Then using Lemma
D.18
, we have

\displaystyle r_{g}

\displaystyle=\inf\limits_{q\neq p\in\mathcal{M}_{g}}\frac{\|q-p\|^{2}}{2d(q-p,\mathcal{T}_{p}(\mathcal{M}_{g}))}\leq\frac{\|g(x)-g(y)\|^{2}}{2d(g(x)-g(y),\mathcal{T}_{g(y)}(\mathcal{M}_{g}))}

\displaystyle=\frac{\|g(x)-g(y)\|}{2}\leq\|g(u^{\star})-g(v^{\star})\|/2.

∎

D.5.2
Extension results

To extend maps that are defined only on a subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{p}
, we will use the following version of Whitney’s extension Theorem (Theorem A in
Fefferman 2005
and "Whitney’s extension Theorem" in
Fefferman 2009
).

Theorem D.20
.

Given
\beta,p\geq 1
, there exists
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
depending only on
p
and
\beta
, for which the following holds. Let
f:E\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with
E
an arbitrary subset of
\mathbb{R}^{p}
. Suppose that for any
k
distinct points
x_{1},...,x_{k}\in E
, there exists
\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1
degree polynomials
P_{1},...,P_{k}
on
\mathbb{R}^{p}
, satisfying

a)

P_{i}(x_{i})=f(x_{i})
for
i=1,...,k
;

b)

\|\nabla^{\alpha}P_{i}(x_{i})\|\leq M
for
i=1,...,k
and
|\alpha|\leq\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1
and

c)

\|\nabla^{\alpha}(P_{i}-P_{j})(x_{i})\|\leq M\|x_{i}-x_{j}\|^{\beta+1-\alpha}
for
i,j=0,...,k
and
|\alpha|\leq\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1
with
M

independent of
x_{1},...,x_{k}

Then
f
extends to
\mathcal{H}_{C}^{\beta+1}(\mathbb{R}^{p},\mathbb{R})
.

Let us now prove a bound on the growth of smooth functions defined on arbitrary subset of
\mathbb{R}^{p}
.

Lemma D.21
.

Let
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
,
x_{1},x_{2}\in E
and
\gamma:[0,1]\rightarrow E
continuous and differentiable almost everywhere with
\gamma(0)=x_{2}
and
\gamma(1)=x_{1}
. Then for all
\eta>0
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
and map
F\in\mathcal{H}^{\eta}_{1}(E,\mathbb{R}^{k})
, we have

\|F(x_{1})-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{\lfloor\eta\rfloor}\nabla^{i}F(x_{2})\frac{(x_{1}-x_{2})^{i}}{i!}\|\leq\|\dot{\gamma}\|_{L^{1}}^{\eta}.

This result is proven by induction. The case
\lfloor\eta\rfloor=0
is straightforward. Let us prove the case
\lfloor\eta\rfloor\geq 1
supposing that the result is true for
\lfloor\eta\rfloor-1
. We have

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle F(x_{1})-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{\lfloor\eta\rfloor}\nabla^{i}F(x_{2})\frac{(x_{1}-x_{2})^{i}}{i!}\|

\displaystyle=\|\int_{0}^{1}\left(\nabla F(\gamma(t))-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{\lfloor\eta\rfloor}\nabla^{i}F(x_{2})\frac{(\gamma(t)-x_{2})^{i-1}}{(i-1)!}\right)\dot{\gamma}(t)dt\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla F(\gamma(\cdot))-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{\lfloor\eta\rfloor-1}\nabla^{i+1}F(x_{2})\frac{(\gamma(\cdot)-x_{2})^{i}}{i!}\|_{\infty}\|\dot{\gamma}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\dot{\gamma}\|^{\eta}_{L^{1}},

where we applied the induction hypothesis to the function

\nabla F
.
∎

E
Proofs of the bound in the manifold case (Section
5.2
)

E.1
Numerical regularity condition

Let us define an easy to compute condition that will ensure that
\hat{g}
verifies the manifold regularity condition.

Definition E.1
.

For
\chi>K
, we say that a map
g\in\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\chi}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})\cap\mathcal{H}^{1}_{K}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})
verifies the
\chi
-numerical regularity condition if for all
z_{i},z_{j}\in\{0,...,[2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}]\}^{d}
with
\|\frac{z_{i}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}}-\frac{z_{j}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}}\|\geq\frac{1}{4\chi^{2}}
, we have
\|g(\frac{z_{i}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}})-g(\frac{z_{j}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}})\|>\frac{1}{8K\chi^{2}}
.

This condition ensures that if two points are far in the torus, then their images by
g
are not too close. The following proposition asserts that the numerical and density regularity conditions together, imply the manifold regularity condition.

Proposition E.2
.

For
\chi>K
, let
g\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{K}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})\cap\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\chi}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})
verify the
\chi
-numerical regularity and
\chi
-density regularity conditions. Then
g
is injective and its image
\mathcal{M}_{g}
has a reach lower bounded by
(C\chi^{5})^{-1}
.

The proof of Proposition
E.2
can be found in Section
E.2
.
Let us now choose the value of the parameter
\chi
that we use for our class of generators. Let
C_{2,\beta+1}>0
such that
\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\beta+1,n^{-\frac{1}{2\tilde{\beta}+d}}}\subset\mathcal{H}^{2}_{C_{2,\beta+1}}
. Note that by Lemma
3.3
, for
\beta+1>2
we have
C_{2,\beta+1}\leq C
and for
\beta+1=2
we have
C_{2,\beta+1}\leq C\log(n)^{2}
. Let
C_{3}>0
be the constant given by Proposition
D.11
such that if
d_{\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1}}(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\leq C_{3}^{-1}
, then
g
verifies the
C_{3}^{-1}
-density regularity condition.
We choose

\chi:=C_{2,\beta+1}\vee C_{3}\vee K.

For
n
large enough, as with high probability we will have that
d_{\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{1}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\leq\chi^{-1}
(see Lemma
E.4
), then
\hat{g}
will verify the assumptions of Proposition
E.2
and therefore it will verify the
(C\chi^{5}\vee\|\hat{g}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}})
-manifold regularity condition.

Note that in practice, the numerical regularity condition of Definition
E.1
can be checked by adding to the loss of the GAN estimator (
1.3
) the term

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}z_{i},z_{j}\in\{0,...,[2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}]\}^{d}\\
\|\frac{z_{i}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}}-\frac{z_{j}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}}\|\geq\frac{1}{4\chi^{2}}\end{subarray}}(

\displaystyle(8K\chi^{2})^{-2}-\|g(\frac{z_{i}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}})-g(\frac{z_{j}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}})\|^{2})

\displaystyle\times\mathds{1}_{\{\|g(\frac{z_{i}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}})-g(\frac{z_{j}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}})\|\leq\frac{1}{8K\chi^{2}}\}},

which is easily differentiable with respect to
g
.

E.2
Proof of proposition
E.2

Let us first show that
g
is injective. As
g
verifies the
\chi
-density regularity condition and belongs to
\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\chi}
, we have for all
u,v\in\mathbb{T}^{d},

\|g(u)-g(v)\|\geq\chi^{-1}\|u-v\|-\chi\|u-v\|^{2}.

Then for
\|u-v\|\leq\frac{1}{2\chi^{2}}
we have,

\|g(u)-g(v)\|\geq\frac{1}{2\chi}\|u-v\|.

Suppose there exists
u,v\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
different such that
g(u)=g(v)
, then from the previous derivation we have
\|u-v\|>\frac{1}{2\chi^{2}}
. Define
z_{u},z_{v}\in\{0,...,[2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}]\}^{d}
such that

\|u-\frac{z_{u}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}}\|+\|v-\frac{z_{v}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}}\|\leq\frac{1}{16K^{2}\chi^{2}}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\|\frac{z_{u}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}}-\frac{z_{v}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}}\|

\displaystyle\geq\|u-v\|-(\|u-\frac{z_{u}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}}\|+\|v-\frac{z_{v}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}}\|)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2\chi^{2}}-\frac{1}{16K^{2}\chi^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{4\chi^{2}}

and

\displaystyle\|g(\frac{z_{u}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}})-g(\frac{z_{v}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}})\|\leq

\displaystyle\|g(u)-g(v)\|

\displaystyle+K(\|u-\frac{z_{u}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}}\|+\|v-\frac{z_{v}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}}\|)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16K\chi^{2}},

which is impossible by definition of the
\chi
-numerical regularity condition.
Therefore, we have that
g
is injective and being a local diffeomorphism, it is a diffeomorphism so in particular
\mathcal{M}_{g}
is a submanifold.

Let us now show that its reach is greater than
(C\chi^{5})^{-1}
. We are going to use the characterisation of the reach for submanifold (Lemma
D.18
). Doing the same derivation as (
D.2.1
), we obtain that for all
u,v\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\|g(u)-g(v)\|^{2}}{2d(g(u)-g(v),\mathcal{T}_{g(v)}(\mathcal{M}_{g}))}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\chi}(\chi^{-2}-\chi^{2}(4\|u-v\|+\|u-v\|^{2})),

so
\|u-v\|\leq\frac{1}{2^{6}\chi^{4}}
implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\|g(u)-g(v)\|^{2}}{2d(g(u)-g(v),\mathcal{T}_{g(v)}(\mathcal{M}_{g}))}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2\chi^{3}}.

Let now consider the case
\|u-v\|\geq\frac{1}{2^{6}\chi^{4}}
. We have

\displaystyle\frac{\|g(u)-g(v)\|^{2}}{2d(g(u)-g(v),\mathcal{T}_{g(v)}(\mathcal{M}_{g}))}\geq\frac{\|g(u)-g(v)\|}{2},

So if
\|u-v\|\in[\frac{1}{2^{6}\chi^{4}},\frac{1}{2\chi^{2}}]
then

\displaystyle\|g(u)-g(v)\|

\displaystyle\geq\chi^{-1}\|u-v\|-\chi\|u-v\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|u-v\|(\chi^{-1}-\chi\|u-v\|)\geq\frac{1}{2^{7}\chi^{5}},

and if
\|u-v\|>\frac{1}{2\chi^{2}}

\displaystyle\|g(u)-g(v)\|

\displaystyle\geq\|g(z_{u})-g(z_{v})\|-K(\|u-\frac{z_{u}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}}\|+\|v-\frac{z_{v}}{2^{5}\sqrt{d}K^{2}\chi^{2}}\|)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{8K\chi^{2}}-\frac{1}{16K^{2}\chi^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{16K\chi^{2}}.

Finally, using Lemma
D.18
we get the result.
∎

E.3
Proof of Theorem
5.4

Recalling the notations of Definition
B.5
for
\gamma=\tilde{\beta}+1
, define
\overline{g}\in\argmin_{g\in\mathcal{G}}L(g,D^{\star}_{g})
.
Doing as in the proof of Theorem
3.1

we obtain that

L(\hat{g},D^{\star}_{\hat{g}})=\Delta^{\hat{g}}_{\mathcal{D}}+L(\hat{g},\overline{D}_{\hat{g}})

and

L(\hat{g},\overline{D}_{\hat{g}})\leq L(\hat{g},\overline{D}_{\hat{g}})-L_{n}(\hat{g},\overline{D}_{\hat{g}})+L_{n}(\overline{g},\hat{D}_{\overline{g}})-L(\overline{g},\hat{D}_{\overline{g}})+\Delta_{\mathcal{G}}.

For
g\in\mathcal{G},
write

\mathcal{D}_{g}:=\{D\circ g\ |\ D\in\mathcal{D}\}.

Recalling the proof of Proposition
B.6
we have for all
\delta>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G},\ D\in\mathcal{D}\\
\|D\circ g\|_{\infty}\leq\delta\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}[D(g(U))]-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(g(U_{i}))\geq\alpha\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{g_{n}\in\mathcal{G}_{1/n}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ D\in\mathcal{D}\\
\|D\circ g_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq\delta+\frac{1}{n}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}[D(g_{n}(U))]-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(g_{n}(U_{i}))\geq\alpha-\frac{2}{n}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{g_{n}\in\mathcal{G}_{1/n}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\exists\ f_{n}\in(\mathcal{D}_{g_{n}})_{1/n}\\
\|f_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq\delta+\frac{2}{n}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}[f_{n}(U))]-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}f_{n}(U_{i}))\geq\alpha-\frac{4}{n}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{g_{n}\in\mathcal{G}_{1/n}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}f_{n}\in\mathcal{D}_{g_{n}1/n}\\
\|f_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq\delta+\frac{2}{n}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathbb{E}[f_{n}(U))]-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}f_{n}(U_{i}))\geq\alpha-\frac{4}{n}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{G}_{1/n}||(\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{G}})_{1/n}|\exp\left(-\frac{n(\alpha-\frac{4}{n})^{2}}{2(\delta+\frac{2}{n})^{2}}\right).

Then solving with respect to
\alpha
the equation
|\mathcal{G}_{1/n}||(\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{G}})_{1/n}|\exp\left(-\frac{n(\alpha-\frac{4}{n})^{2}}{2(2K)^{2}}\right)=1/n
, we obtain that with probability at least
1/n

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{G},\ D\in\mathcal{D}\\
\|D\circ g\|_{\infty}\leq\delta\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}[D(g(U))]-\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}D(g(U_{i}))

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\frac{(\delta+1/n)^{2}\log(n|\mathcal{G}_{1/n}||(\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{G}})_{1/n}|)}{n}}+\frac{1}{n}.

Now doing as in the proof of Theorem
3.1
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[L(\hat{g},\overline{D}_{\hat{g}}))\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\min\limits_{\delta\in[0,1]}\sqrt{\frac{(\delta+1/n)^{2}\log(n|\mathcal{G}_{1/n}||(\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{G}})_{1/n}|)}{n}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}(1+\delta^{(1-\frac{d}{2(\beta+1)})}\mathds{1}_{\{\beta+1<d/2\}}+\log(\delta^{-1})\mathds{1}_{\{\beta+1\geq d/2\}})+\Delta_{\mathcal{G}}.

(E.1)

∎

E.4
Proof of Lemma
5.5

Supposing that
\delta^{-1}K
is an integer, for
(k_{1},...,k_{d})\in\{0,...,2\delta^{-1}K-1\}^{d}
let

u^{k_{1},...,k_{d}}=(k_{1}\frac{\delta}{2K},...,k_{d}\frac{\delta}{2K})

and

A_{k_{1},...,k_{d}}=[u^{k_{1},...,k_{d}}_{1},u^{k_{1},...,k_{d}}_{1}+\frac{\delta}{2K}]\times...\times[u^{k_{1},...,k_{d}}_{d},u^{k_{1},...,k_{d}}_{d}+\frac{\delta}{2K}]\subset[0,1]^{d}.

For
D\in\mathcal{F}^{\tilde{\beta}+1,\delta}
, we have by definition of
\mathcal{D}
that there exist weights

\alpha_{D}(j,l,w)\in[-2^{-j(\tilde{\beta}+1+p/2)},2^{-j(\tilde{\beta}+1+p/2)}]
such that

D(x)=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\log_{2}(\lfloor\delta^{-1}\rfloor)}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{p}}\sum\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}\alpha_{D}(j,l,w)\hat{\psi}_{jlw}(x)

As
supp(\hat{\psi}_{jlw})\subset\bigotimes\limits_{i=1}^{p}[2^{-j}(w_{i}-C),2^{-j}(w_{i}+C)]
and
g
is
K
-Lipschitz, we have for all
i\in\{1,...,p\}
and
j\leq\log_{2}(\lfloor\delta^{-1}\rfloor)
,

supp(\hat{\psi}_{jlw})\cap g_{i}(A_{k_{1},...,k_{d}})\neq\varnothing

implies

[2^{-j}(w_{i}-C),2^{-j}(w_{i}+C)]\cap[g_{i}(u^{k_{1},...,k_{d}})-\delta/2,g_{i}(u^{k_{1},...,k_{d}})+\delta/2]\neq\varnothing,

which implies

2^{j}(g_{i}(u^{k_{1},...,k_{d}})-\delta/2)-C\leq w_{i}\leq 2^{j}(g_{i}(u^{k_{1},...,k_{d}})+\delta/2)+C

and as
|2^{j-1}\delta|<1
they are at most
2+2C
integers
w_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
supp(\hat{\psi}_{jlw})\cap g_{i}(A_{k_{1},...,k_{d}})\neq\varnothing
. Therefore,
D_{|g(A_{k_{1},...,k_{d}})}
the restriction of
D
to
g(A_{k_{1},...,k_{d}})
can be represented by
\log(\delta^{-1})(2^{p}(2+2C))^{p}
weights and so
D_{|g([0,1]^{d})}
can be represented by
(2K\delta^{-1})^{d}\log(\delta^{-1})(2^{p}(2+2C))^{p}
weights.

We can then conclude that
\forall\epsilon\in(0,1),

\log(|(\mathcal{N}_{g})_{\epsilon}|)\leq\log((C^{-1}\epsilon)^{-(2K\delta^{-1})^{d}\log(\delta^{-1})(2^{p}(2+C))^{p}})\leq C\delta^{-d}\log(\delta^{-1})\log(\epsilon^{-1}).

∎

E.5
Proof of Lemma
5.6

Let
\pi_{g}
be an optimal transport plan for the Euclidean distance between
g_{\#U}
and
g^{\star}_{\#U}
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[

\displaystyle D_{g}^{\star}(g(U))-D_{g}^{\star}(g^{\star}(U))-(\overline{D}_{g}(g(U))-\overline{D}_{g}(g^{\star}(U)))]

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}}\left(D_{g}^{\star}(x)-D_{g}^{\star}(y)-(\overline{D}_{g}(x)-\overline{D}_{g}(y))\right)d\pi_{g}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}}\int_{0}^{1}\Big\langle\nabla D_{g}^{\star}(y+t(x-y))-\nabla\overline{D}_{g}(y+t(x-y)),x-y\Big\rangle dtd\pi_{g}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla D_{g}^{\star}-\nabla\overline{D}_{g}\|_{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}}\|x-y\|d\pi_{g}(x,y)

\displaystyle=C\|\nabla D_{g}^{\star}-\nabla\overline{D}_{g}\|_{\infty}\sup\limits_{f\in\text{Lip}_{1}}\mathbb{E}[f(g(U))-f(g^{\star}(U))].

Writing

\mathcal{K}=\|g\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\tilde{\beta}+1}},

we have
\mathcal{K}\leq C\log(n)^{2}
from Proposition
E.3
. Using Theorem
5.1
with
\epsilon=1/n
we obtain

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{f\in\text{Lip}_{1}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[f(g(U))-f(g^{\star}(U))]

\displaystyle\leq(2K+1)\sup\limits_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{1}}\mathbb{E}[f(g(U))-f(g^{\star}(U))]

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{K}\chi)^{C_{2}}\left(\log(n)^{4}\sup\limits_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\tilde{\beta}+1}_{1}}\mathbb{E}[f(g(U))-f(g^{\star}(U))]^{\frac{\tilde{\beta}+1}{\tilde{\beta}+\tilde{\beta}+1}}+\frac{1}{n}\right)

∎

E.6
Proof of Theorem
5.7

The proof breaks down as follow. First, we determine the smoothness of the functions in the class of generators
\mathcal{G}
and show that with high probability,
\hat{g}
verifies the density and manifold regularity conditions. Then, we bound the term of approximation error of the generator class. Finally we prove that the GAN estimator attains the minimax rate for the distance
d_{\mathcal{H}^{\tilde{\beta}+1}_{1}}
using Theorem
5.4
.

E.6.1
Regularity of
\hat{g}

Let us first determine the regularity of the functions in the class
\mathcal{G}
.

Proposition E.3
.

For all
\eta\in(0,\beta+1]
and
\delta\in(0,1)
, we have that if
\eta
is not an integer then

\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\eta,\delta}_{per}\subset\mathcal{H}^{\eta}_{C},

and if
\eta
is an integer

\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\eta,\delta}_{per}\subset\mathcal{H}^{\eta}_{C\log(\delta^{-1})^{2}}.

Let
f\in\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\eta,\delta}_{per}
, then from Proposition
A.3
there exist coefficients
(\alpha_{f}(j,l,z))
such that

f=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\alpha_{f}(j,l,z)\psi_{jlz}^{per},

with

|\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)|\leq(C_{\eta}K+C\delta^{\eta+\gamma})2^{-j(\eta+d/2)},\text{ for }j\leq\log_{2}(\delta^{-1})

and

|\alpha_{f}(j,l,w)|\leq C2^{-j(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2+d/2)}\delta^{\eta+\gamma},\text{ for }j>\log_{2}(\delta^{-1}).

Then if
\eta
is not an integer, we have
\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\eta}}\leq C
as
\mathcal{B}^{\eta}_{\infty,\infty}\subset\mathcal{H}^{\eta}
. Now if
\eta
is an integer we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\eta}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\eta,2}_{\infty,\infty}}=C\sup\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}2^{j(\eta+d/2)}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sup\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}(1+j)^{2}|\alpha_{f}(j,l,z)|

\displaystyle\leq C\sup\limits_{j\leq\log_{2}(\delta^{-1})}2^{j(\eta+d/2)}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sup\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}(1+j)^{2}|\alpha_{f}(j,l,z)|+C\delta^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2-\eta}\log(\delta^{-1})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sup\limits_{j\leq\log(\delta^{-1}))}(1+j)^{2}+C\delta^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2-\eta}\log(\delta^{-1})^{2}\leq C\log(\delta^{-1})^{2}.

∎Using Proposition
E.3
, we have that if
\beta
is not an integer then
\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\beta+1,n^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+d}}}_{per}\subset\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{C},

and if
\beta
is an integer
\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\beta+1,n^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+d}}}_{per}\subset\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{C\log(n)^{2}}.

Using this result, let us give a first (sub-optimal) bound on
d_{\mathcal{H}^{\tilde{\beta}+1}_{1}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})
that will allow us to use the different results that require the distance
d_{\mathcal{H}^{\tilde{\beta}+1}_{1}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})
to be small.

Lemma E.4
.

For all
g^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{K}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})
, the GAN estimator
\hat{g}
of (
1.3
) with
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{D}
defined in (
5.1
) , verifies that with probability at least
1/n
we have

d_{\mathcal{H}^{\tilde{\beta}+1}_{1}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\leq Cn^{-\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{2{\beta}+d}}.

We are going to use the bound given by Theorem
5.4
.
Using Lemma
5.5
we have that
\log(|(\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{G}})_{1/n}|)\leq C\log(n)^{2}n^{\frac{d}{2\tilde{\beta}+d}}
, and from Proposition
3.7
we have
|\mathcal{G}_{1/n}|\leq C\log(n)^{2}n^{\frac{d}{2\beta+d}}
.
On the other hand, from Proposition
E.5
we have that
\Delta_{\mathcal{G}}\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-\frac{\beta+\tilde{\beta}+1}{2\tilde{\beta}+d}}
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\Delta^{\hat{g}}_{\mathcal{D}}]

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{\mathcal{D}}=\sup\limits_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\bigl\{d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\tilde{\beta}+1}}(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})-\Big(\sup\limits_{D\in\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{E}_{U\sim\mathcal{U}([0,1]^{d})}[D(g(U))-D(g^{\star}(U))]\Big)\bigl\}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{D^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\tilde{\beta}+1}(B^{p}(0,K),\mathbb{R})}\inf\limits_{D\in\mathcal{D}}\|D-D^{\star}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{D^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\tilde{\beta}+1}(B^{p}(0,K),\mathbb{R})}\inf\limits_{D\in\mathcal{D}}\|D-D^{\star}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{0,2}_{\infty,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{2}n^{-\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{2\tilde{\beta}+d}}.

Finally, taking
\delta=1
and putting everything in the bound of Theorem
5.4
, we get the result.
∎As discussed Section
E.1
, using Lemma
E.4
and Proposition
E.2
for
n
large enough, we have that
\hat{g}
verifies the
(C\chi^{5}\vee\|\hat{g}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}})
-manifold regularity condition.

E.6.2
Approximation error of the class
\mathcal{G}

From Proposition
3.7
, we know that

\inf_{g\in\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\beta+1,n^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+d}}}_{per}}d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\tilde{\beta}+1}}(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-\frac{\beta+\tilde{\beta}+1}{2\tilde{\beta}+d}}.

(E.2)

Therefore it suffices to prove that there exists
\overline{g}\in\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\beta+1,n^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+d}}}_{per}
that verifies both (
E.2
) and the
\chi
-numerical regularity condition. We show in the proof of the next result that the map being the low wavelet frequency approximation of
g^{\star}
by neural networks, verifies these conditions.

Proposition E.5
.

For all
g^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta+1}_{K}(\mathbb{T}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})
that verifies the
K
-density regularity condition of Definition
2.1
, the class
\mathcal{G}
defined in (
5.1
) verifies

\Delta_{\mathcal{G}}=\inf_{g\in\mathcal{G}}d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\tilde{\beta}+1}}(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-\frac{\beta+\tilde{\beta}+1}{2\tilde{\beta}+d}}.

Let

\overline{g}(u)=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{n^{\frac{1}{2\beta+d}}}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\alpha_{g^{\star}}(j,l,z)\hat{\psi}_{jlz}^{per}(u),

for
\alpha_{g^{\star}}(j,l,z)=\langle g^{\star},\psi_{jlz}^{per}\rangle
. Then, from Proposition
A.3
there exist coefficients
(\alpha_{\overline{g}}(j,l,z))
such that

\overline{g}=\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sum\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}\alpha_{\overline{g}}(j,l,z)\psi_{jlz}^{per},

with

|\alpha_{\overline{g}}(j,l,w)-\alpha_{g^{\star}}(j,l,w)|\leq Cn^{-\frac{\beta+\tilde{\beta}+2}{2\beta+d}}2^{-j(\beta+1+d/2)},\text{ for }j\leq\log_{2}(n^{\frac{1}{2\beta+d}})

and

|\alpha_{\overline{g}}(j,l,w)|\leq C2^{-j(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2+d/2)}n^{-\frac{\beta+\tilde{\beta}+2}{2\beta+d}},\text{ for }j>\log_{2}(n^{\frac{1}{2\beta+d}}).

Let
u,v\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
with
\|u-v\|\geq(4\chi^{2})^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\overline{g}(u)-\overline{g}(v)\|\geq\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|-2\|\overline{g}-g^{\star}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|-2C\|\overline{g}-g^{\star}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{0,2}_{\infty,\infty}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|-C\sup\limits_{j\geq 0}2^{jd/2}(1+j)^{2}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sup\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}|\alpha_{\overline{g}}(j,l,w)-\alpha_{g^{\star}}(j,l,w)|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|-C\Big(\sup\limits_{j\leq\log_{2}(n^{\frac{1}{2\beta+d}})}(1+j)^{2}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sup\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}n^{-\frac{\beta+\tilde{\beta}+2}{2\beta+d}}2^{-j(\beta+1)}

\displaystyle+\sup\limits_{j>\log_{2}(n^{\frac{1}{2\beta+d}})}(1+j)^{2}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sup\limits_{z\in\{0,...,2^{j}-1\}^{d}}(CK+n^{-\frac{\beta+\tilde{\beta}+2}{2\beta+d}})2^{-j(\beta+1)}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|-C\log(n)^{2}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}.

Using Lemma
D.19
with
\delta=(4\chi^{2})^{-1}
, we have that if

\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|\leq K^{-1}(4\chi^{2})^{-1}-K(4\chi^{2})^{-2},

then

r_{g^{\star}}\leq\frac{\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|}{2}.

Furtermore,

\displaystyle K^{-1}(4\chi^{2})^{-1}-K(4\chi^{2})^{-2}

\displaystyle=(4\chi^{2})^{-1}(K^{-1}-K(4\chi^{2})^{-1})

\displaystyle\geq(4\chi^{2})^{-1}(K^{-1}-(4K)^{-1})=\frac{3}{4}(4K\chi^{2})^{-1},

as
\chi\geq K
. Then if
\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|\leq\frac{3}{4}(4K\chi^{2})^{-1}
, we would have
r_{g^{\star}}\leq\frac{3}{8}(4K\chi^{2})^{-1}<K^{-1}
, which is impossible as
g^{\star}
verifies the
K
-manifold regularity condition. Therefore we deduce that

\displaystyle\|\overline{g}(u)-\overline{g}(v)\|

\displaystyle\geq\|g^{\star}(u)-g^{\star}(v)\|-C\log(n)^{2}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{3}{4}(4K\chi^{2})^{-1}-C\log(n)^{2}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}>(8K\chi^{2})^{-1},

for n large enough.

We conclude that
\overline{g}
verifies the
\chi
-numerical condition and thus belongs to
\mathcal{G}
. Finally we can show like in the Proof of Proposition
3.6
Section
B.3.2
, that

d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\tilde{\beta}+1}}(\overline{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-\frac{\beta+\tilde{\beta}+1}{2\tilde{\beta}+d}}.

∎

E.6.3
Putting everything together

We now give the proof of Theorem
5.7
.

By stability of Besov spaces for the differentiation (proposition 4.3.19,
Giné and Nickl 2015
) we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla D_{g}^{\star}-\nabla\overline{D}_{g}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla D_{g}^{\star}-\nabla\overline{D}_{g}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{0,2}_{\infty,\infty}}\leq C\|D_{g}^{\star}-\overline{D}_{g}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{1,2}_{\infty,\infty}}

\displaystyle=C\sup\limits_{j\geq\log_{2}(n^{-\frac{1}{2\tilde{\beta}+d}})}2^{j(1+p/2)}(1+j)^{2}\sum\limits_{l=1}^{2^{d}}\sup\limits_{w\in\mathbb{Z}^{p}}|\alpha_{D_{g}^{\star}}(j,l,z)|

\displaystyle\leq C\sup\limits_{j\geq\log_{2}(n^{-\frac{1}{2\tilde{\beta}+d}})}2^{-j\tilde{\beta}}(1+j)^{2}

\displaystyle=Cn^{-\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{2\tilde{\beta}+d}}\log(n)^{2}.

Therefore, by Lemma
5.6
, we have

\Delta_{\mathcal{D}}^{g}\leq C\log(n)^{C_{3}}n^{-\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{2\tilde{\beta}+d}}\Big(d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\tilde{\beta}+1}}(g_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})^{\frac{\tilde{\beta}+1}{2\tilde{\beta}+1}}+\frac{1}{n}\Big).

Suppose first that
\beta+1<d/2
.
Then, using the bounds on the approximation errors and covering numbers derived previously, for
\delta=n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}
we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle+\min\limits_{\delta\in[0,1]}\Biggl\{\sqrt{\frac{(\delta+1/n)^{2}\log(n|\mathcal{G}_{1/n}||(\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{G}})_{1/n}|)}{n}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}(1+\delta^{(1-\frac{d}{2(\beta+1)})})\Biggl\}

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-\frac{2\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}.

Then, using Theorem
5.4
and Lemma
5.6
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\beta+1}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\Big]\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\Delta^{\hat{g}}_{\mathcal{D}}]+C\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-\frac{2\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\log(n)^{C_{3}}n^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}\left(\mathbb{E}\Big[d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\beta+1}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\Big]^{\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+1}}+n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}\right).

Now if
\mathbb{E}\Big[d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\beta+1}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\Big]\leq n^{-\frac{2\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}
we have the result, otherwise

n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\beta+1}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\Big]^{\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+1}}

so

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\beta+1}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{3}}n^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}\left(2\mathbb{E}\Big[d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\beta+1}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\Big]^{\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+1}}\right),

so

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\beta+1}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\Big]^{\frac{\beta}{2\beta+1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{3}}n^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}

which gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\beta+1}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{3}}n^{-\frac{2\beta+1}{2\beta+d}}.

Let us treat now the case
\beta+1\geq d/2
. For
\delta=n^{-\frac{d/2}{2\tilde{\beta}+d}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle+\min\limits_{\delta\in[0,1]}\Biggl\{\sqrt{\frac{(\delta+1/n)^{2}\log(n|\mathcal{G}_{1/n}||(\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{G}})_{1/n}|)}{n}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}(1+\log(\delta^{-1}))\Biggl\}

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{2}}n^{-\frac{1}{2}},

and as for the case
\beta+1<d/2
, using Theorem
5.4
and Lemma
5.6
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{d/2}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{3}}n^{-\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{2\tilde{\beta}+d}}\left(\mathbb{E}\Big[d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{d/2}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\Big]^{\frac{\tilde{\beta}+1}{2\tilde{\beta}+1}}+n^{-\frac{\tilde{\beta}+1}{2\tilde{\beta}+d}}\right).

Now if
\mathbb{E}\Big[d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{d/2}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\Big]\leq n^{-\frac{1}{2}}
we have the result, otherwise

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{d/2}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{3}}n^{-\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{2\tilde{\beta}+d}}\mathbb{E}\Big[d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{d/2}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\Big]^{\frac{\tilde{\beta}+1}{2\tilde{\beta}+1}}.

We conclude the same way by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{d/2}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\Big]^{\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{2\tilde{\beta}+1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{3}}n^{-\frac{\tilde{\beta}}{2\tilde{\beta}+d}}

which gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[d_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{d/2}}(\hat{g}_{\#U},g^{\star}_{\#U})\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)^{C_{3}}n^{-\frac{2\tilde{\beta}+1}{2\tilde{\beta}+d}}=C\log(n)^{C_{3}}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

∎
